# API Authorization Matrix

> Phase 6 — API Design · Cross-cutting authorization reconciliation for every route in the API Master Index.
> Status of this document: see §21. This document introduces **no** new permission, role, credential type, route, status code, error code, migration, or authorization mechanism. It is a derivation. Where the baseline sources conflict, it resolves the conflict by the precedence in §2 or applies a recorded owner decision (§15.3).

## 1. Purpose and Scope

### 1.1 Purpose

This document gives one authoritative, row-by-row answer to **"which principal, presenting which credential, may invoke which route, under which condition, and with which failure status"** for every route in the AMI. The answers come from the executed grants, not from any prose role table. This lets security tests be generated mechanically (§17). It also surfaces, classifies and closes inconsistencies between the baseline contracts, either by precedence or by a recorded owner decision.

### 1.2 In scope

- All 369 AMI §5 rows (one matrix row each, §13) and all 4 AMI §6 WebSocket rows (§14).
- All five tenant system roles, custom roles, API keys, platform admin (with and without break-glass), internal service principals, anonymous callers, providers/callback senders, and restricted-purpose tokens.
- Tenant isolation, platform-admin separation, break-glass (including route-by-route purpose classification, §9), sensitive media, internal-service trust and callback trust.

### 1.3 Out of scope (explicitly)

- Any change to roles, permissions, grants, routes, error codes or migrations. There is no migration 113. The only contract text changes are the owner-authorised, append-only amendments in §15.5.
- OpenAPI/Swagger/FastAPI artefacts, application code, and test code in the application repository.
- Error-catalogue and versioning documents. This pass does not create them.
- Pricing, quotas, rate limits and plan entitlements. They appear in the Guards column only as **non-authorization** controls (§7.6).
- Frontend behaviour. No UI hiding, disabled button or client-side check is ever treated as a control here.

---

## 2. Authority and Precedence

When two sources disagree, the higher-ranked source wins. A lower source is never used to widen a grant that a higher one made or withheld.

| Rank | Source | Role in this document |
|---|---|---|
| A | **Executed migrations**: `007_5B.sql` (5 system roles, base permission catalogue and grants), `096_5B2.sql` (`crm_field:manage`), `104_5B3.sql` (`recording:access_media`, `transcript:access_content`), `105_5B4`/`107_5B5` (break-glass purposes, `fn_break_glass_check`, `is_platform_admin()` binding) | Sole source of the role × permission grants (§6). Parsed, never transcribed. |
| B | **FAR** decisions (Table 1 rules #9–#15; status-code table #8–#10) | Binding. Not reopened. |
| C | **AMI** §5/§6 | Sole source of the route set, route IDs, required permission, auth cell and tenant cell. |
| D | **Owning contracts 6A–6M**, including the controlled amendments of §15.5 | Per-route credential eligibility, guards, callback mechanisms and internal-route rules. |
| E | **6B global model** (credential types, §16 key ceiling, §17 token routing, §18 platform admin/break-glass, §22 codes) | Default where D is silent. |
| F | Older Phase 1–5 prose | Used only where not superseded. The 9-role DDD model (6B ADR-6B-08) is superseded and is **not** used anywhere in this document. |

Consequences applied throughout:

1. A contract test table or prose role list that disagrees with the executed grants is a documentation defect in the lower source (ledger, §15). It never creates a grant.
2. The AMI permission string is copied exactly, and AND/OR structure is preserved. No permission is ever replaced by a broader one.
3. Where A–E are silent and more than one materially different safe answer exists, the matter goes to an owner decision (§15.3). This document does not choose.

---

## 3. Authorization Model and Cell Vocabulary

### 3.1 Model

Authorization for a request is the conjunction of independent gates, evaluated in this order. The first gate that fails determines the status.

1. **Credential gate.** The presented credential must be a type accepted at this surface (§4). It must be valid (signature, `iss`/`aud`/`exp`/`nbf`, `token_use`) and not revoked or expired. Failure → the `401 AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED` family (FAR code #8; 6B §22: `AUTHENTICATION_REQUIRED`, `TOKEN_EXPIRED`, `TOKEN_REVOKED`, `INVALID_API_KEY`).
2. **Principal-boundary gate.** The principal class must be permitted on this surface (tenant vs platform-admin vs internal vs callback).
   - Tenant principal on a platform-admin route → `403 TENANT_PRINCIPAL_FORBIDDEN` (6M §28).
   - Non-PA principal on a PA-only route → `403 PLATFORM_ADMIN_REQUIRED` (6M §28).
   - API key on a key-ineligible tenant route → `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` (FAR code #9, rule #9; see AAM-F-04).
3. **Tenant gate.** The target resource must belong to the organization bound to the credential. Failure → `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, never 403 (§8).
4. **Permission gate.** The effective permission set must satisfy the route's exact permission expression. Failure → `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`. A PLATFORM_ADMIN holds no tenant permission and never passes this gate on its own; on a read-oriented tenant route its request is decided by gate 5 instead, and on a tenant write it fails here.
5. **Break-glass gate** (PA on tenant data only). It is additional, never a substitute. It applies to every PA request on a read-oriented tenant route (§9) and on AMI-6M-008/009, whether or not `X-Break-Glass-Grant` is presented. A missing header, a grant that fails any check, or a wrong purpose → `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` (FAR L261 code #10, which lists "missing header" among the collapsed failures; 6B L911, L960, L2083; AAM-F-07). If Redis is unavailable → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, which denies access and never allows it.
6. **Guards.** Row-specific non-RBAC checks (ownership, author, last-owner, state, custom-role subset). They can reject a request that passed RBAC, with the owning contract's status (e.g. `409 LAST_OWNER_PROTECTED`, `409 STATE_CONFLICT`, `409 ROLE_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` for an OWNER target, or `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` for the custom-role subset rule). They never grant.

Quotas, rate limits and plan entitlements are **not** gates of this model (§7.6).

### 3.2 Cell vocabulary (the only values used in §13 and §14)

| Value | Exact meaning |
|---|---|
| `ALLOW` | A principal of this column, presenting the credential in the Principal/credential column, **passes every authorization gate** for this route in its own tenant. Guards in the Guards column and state conflicts can still reject; neither is authorization. |
| `ALLOW (ANON)` | The route is pre-authentication, so an unauthenticated caller passes authorization. Rate limits still apply, and they are not authorization. |
| `DENY 401` | Rejected at the credential gate: the credential is absent or not accepted on this surface. Code family per 6B §22 / FAR code #8. `DENY 401 (ANON)` = no credential presented. `DENY 401 (human PA token)` = a human platform-admin access token presented to an internal-only route. |
| `DENY 403` | Rejected with `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`. Either the principal is authenticated and in-tenant but lacks the exact required permission, or (for API keys) the route is key-ineligible. In the PLATFORM_ADMIN column it means the route is outside break-glass scope (a tenant write, or AMI-6D-011/014): the result is `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` whether or not a grant is presented. |
| `DENY 403 TPF` | Rejected with `403 TENANT_PRINCIPAL_FORBIDDEN` at the platform-admin router, before any database call (6M §28). |
| `DENY SIG` | Callback routes only. A bearer credential of this column is **not a recognised credential** on this surface. It is ignored, and the request is authenticated solely by the provider signature or the OAuth `state`. If that verification fails, the request fails with the owning contract's failure status. This document invents no status code. |
| `CONDITIONAL <codes>` | The outcome depends on the enumerated condition(s) defined in §7.3. Codes joined with `+` must **all** hold (AND). If any fails, the result is the failure status defined for that code. |
| `N/A` | The principal class has no meaning on this route (e.g. a tenant role on a pre-authentication route, where no tenant context exists yet). `N/A` is never a hidden allow: presenting such a credential grants no additional capability. |

Blank cells are not permitted (Gate C).

---

## 4. Principal Taxonomy

| Principal / credential | What it is | What it is not | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Tenant user — system role** `OWNER`, `ADMIN`, `MEMBER`, `BILLING_ADMIN`, `VIEWER` | A human with an ACTIVE membership, authenticated by a user access token (`token_use=access`, RS256 user-facing key, denylist-checked). Its permissions are the parsed grants of §6. | Not a platform admin. No role inherits from another at runtime; the permission set is exactly the granted rows. | 007_5B L12–19; 6B §6.1, §10 |
| **Tenant user — custom role** | A tenant-defined role whose effective permissions are exactly its `role_permissions` rows. It is a role with `is_system = false`. After AAM-OD-02 A, its permissions are always a subset of what its creator, editor or assigner could exercise, except that OWNER may use the whole 70-permission tenant catalogue. The subset rule applies to custom roles only; system roles are assigned under the frozen hierarchy (§7.1). | Never inherits from a system role, is never "ADMIN-like" by name, cannot be OWNER, and never confers PLATFORM_ADMIN identity or any platform-only capability. | 5B §37.6; 6B §21.31/§21.33 (as amended); 6C §8.4/§15.16 (as amended) |
| **API key** | An org-bound credential (`identity.api_keys`) whose subject is `api_key_id`. Effective permissions = `scopes` ∩ the issuer's permissions at issuance. Scopes are a ceiling, never a grant. | Not a user. It has no membership, no identity for `/me`-style self routes (AAM-OD-01 A), and no platform-admin authority. A revoked or expired key → 401. | 6B §16.4; FAR #9, #13; 6C §17 |
| **PLATFORM_ADMIN** | A human operator whose access token carries the platform-admin claim. It is served by the `app_platform_admin` DB role (`is_platform_admin()` = `session_user='app_platform_admin'` AND GUC, 107_5B5). | **Not a tenant role.** It does not satisfy tenant RBAC and is disjoint from `ADMIN` (FAR #12). | 6B §18; 6M §27–§28 |
| **Break-glass grant** | A time-boxed, purpose-scoped grant (`purposes`, 105_5B4) bound to one organization, one admin and one session, presented via `X-Break-Glass-Grant`. On tenant routes it is scoped to reads, and each read route requires a mapped purpose (AAM-OD-03 C, §9). | An **additional gate** on PA access to tenant data. It is never a substitute for PA, never a tenant permission, and never a write authority. | 6B §18.3a/§18.3b (as amended)/§18.4; 6M §11–§14; FAR #14 |
| **INTERNAL_SERVICE** | A non-human service principal authenticated purely by internal-JWT signature (central internal issuer key, `token_use=internal`, `service_id`, allow-list, optional `on_behalf_of_organization_id`). | It has no permission set and is not a user. It is accepted only on `/api/internal/v1/*`. | 6B L108–154; FAR #10 |
| **Provider / callback sender** | An external system authenticated by a provider-native signature or an OAuth `state` value. | Not a tenant principal. The tenant is resolved only after verification. | FAR #11; 6D §10.4; 6J; 6K §30 |
| **Restricted-purpose tokens** | `mfa_challenge` (accepted only at `POST /auth/mfa/verify`), `login_continuation` (accepted only at `/auth/organization/select`), the refresh token (only at refresh), and single-use body tokens (email verification, password reset, invitation). | **Not access tokens.** They are rejected everywhere except their designated endpoint. | 6B §10.1–§10.2, L152–154, L290 |
| **Anonymous** | No credential. | — | 6B §22 |

## 5. Permission Catalogue (71 permissions, parsed from executed SQL)

The catalogue is the union of the permission INSERTs in `007_5B.sql` (L22), `096_5B2.sql` (L34) and `104_5B3.sql` (L78). The UPDATEs at `104_5B3.sql` L88/L92 change display names only. No migration deletes a permission or a grant. "§13 rows requiring it" is a computed count of the §13 rows whose permission expression names the permission.

| # | Permission | Resource | Action | Display name (seed) | Seeded by | Held by (system roles) | §13 rows requiring it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `organization:read` | organization | read | View Organization | 007_5B.sql | OWNER, ADMIN, MEMBER, BILLING_ADMIN, VIEWER | 6 |
| 2 | `organization:update` | organization | update | Edit Organization Settings | 007_5B.sql | OWNER, ADMIN | 9 |
| 3 | `organization:suspend` | organization | suspend | Suspend Organization | 007_5B.sql | OWNER | 1 |
| 4 | `organization:delete` | organization | delete | Delete Organization | 007_5B.sql | OWNER | 1 |

Observations (all computed from the table above):

- `analytics_platform:read` is granted to **no** tenant role; it is platform-internal (6L §11; 6M-031). OWNER's tenant catalogue under the AAM-OD-02 subset rule is therefore the 70 remaining permissions.
- `ADMIN` = `OWNER` minus `organization:suspend`, `organization:delete`, `billing:manage` and `tax:manage`. These four are exactly the permissions that the subset rule (AAM-OD-02 A) stops an ADMIN from placing in a custom role.
- `recording:access_media` and `transcript:access_content` are granted to `OWNER` and `ADMIN` only.
- `api_key:read` and `api_key:manage` are granted to `OWNER` and `ADMIN` only (basis of AAM-F-01).

## 7. Evaluation Rules

### 7.1 Role cells (RBAC rows)

For every RBAC row, a system-role cell is `ALLOW` if and only if every permission in the row's AND-expression is in that role's parsed grant set (§6). Otherwise it is `DENY 403`. There is no other input, apart from the row-specific expressions and the subset guard below.

| Row | Expression (exact) | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| AMI-6C-009 | `member:read` | AMI cell "same as §15.8 (member:read)" |
| AMI-6C-015 | `organization:update` AND caller is current OWNER | Identity-bound (6C §8.5, §17): only the current OWNER. ADMIN, custom role and key → 403. |
| AMI-6C-017 | `member:invite` AND `member:read` | AND preserved (AAM-I-03) |
| AMI-6D-004 | `call:transfer` | Interim mapping DEP-6D-01; string preserved |
| AMI-6G-044, AMI-6G-055 | `contact:write` (contact target) OR `deal:write` (deal target) | The OR is **by target**, not "either one for anything". A role holding both → `ALLOW`; neither → `DENY 403`; exactly one → `CONDITIONAL C-TARGET` (computed; no system role holds exactly one). |
| AMI-6G-061 | `contact:write` + author/role guard | DEP-6G-02 interim. OWNER/ADMIN may delete any note. Other `contact:write` holders may delete their own or AI-generated notes (`C-AUTHOR`). Without `contact:write` → 403. |
| AMI-6A-001 | AAM-OD-04 C (6A §18.3 L488, as amended) and the generic-job registry (§7.3b) | Missing or foreign job → 404. The server resolves the job's canonical type and loads its registry entry; allowed only if the entry supports an initiator and the caller is that initiator (exact principal type and id), **or** the caller holds the entry's `view_permission`; otherwise 403. No generic fallback; unknown type → denied (`C-JOB`). PLATFORM_ADMIN never satisfies `C-JOB`; it reads only via `C-BG-R-GENERAL`. |
| AMI-6D-011 | `recording:access_media` AND `recording:read` | Sensitive media (§12) |
| AMI-6D-014 | `transcript:access_content` AND `transcript:read` | Sensitive media (§12) |
| AMI-6H-025 | AND as in AMI | 6H L808 |
| AMI-6B-029, AMI-6B-031 | as in AMI (`role:manage`) **plus** the custom-role subset rule (AAM-OD-02 A). These routes create or edit custom roles only (`is_system = true` roles reject mutation). | OWNER → `ALLOW` (unrestricted within the tenant catalogue). ADMIN → `CONDITIONAL C-SUBSET`. Custom role → `CONDITIONAL C-CR+C-SUBSET`. Key → `DENY 403` (D1). Roles lacking `role:manage` → `DENY 403`. |
| AMI-6C-010, AMI-6C-016 | as in AMI (`role:manage` / `member:invite`) **plus** the attach rule `C-ATTACH` | The attached role may be a system or a custom role. System role: the frozen hierarchy (ADMIN may assign or invite any non-OWNER system role, including `BILLING_ADMIN`; OWNER is never assigned or invited here). Custom role: the subset rule. OWNER → `ALLOW`. ADMIN → `CONDITIONAL C-ATTACH`. Custom role → `CONDITIONAL C-CR+C-ATTACH`. Key: 6C-010 → `DENY 403` (D1); 6C-016 → `CONDITIONAL C-KEY+C-ATTACH`. Roles lacking the base permission → `DENY 403`. |

### 7.2 Other columns

| Column | Rule on RBAC rows |
|---|---|
| CUSTOM_ROLE | `CONDITIONAL C-CR`, plus the row's other condition codes. Never `ALLOW` by analogy with a system role. |
| API_KEY | `DENY 403` if the owning contract marks the route key-ineligible (list D1, §7.5). `CONDITIONAL C-KEY-SM` on AMI-6D-011/014. Otherwise `CONDITIONAL C-KEY`. |
| PLATFORM_ADMIN | **Read-oriented rows** (every `GET` plus AMI-6I-006, excluding AMI-6D-011/014) → `CONDITIONAL C-BG-R-<PURPOSE>`, with the purpose mapped route by route (§9.2). **Every other RBAC row** (tenant writes) → `DENY 403`: the result is `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` even with a valid grant (6B §18.4 L1006; §31 L2343). **AMI-6D-011/014** → `DENY 403`; PA reaches media only via AMI-6M-008/009. |
| INTERNAL_SERVICE | `DENY 401` (6C §17: internal token on a public route → 401) |
| OTHER / ANON | `DENY 401 (ANON)` |

### 7.3 Condition codes

| Code | Condition (all must hold) | On failure |
|---|---|---|
| `C-KEY` | The key is ACTIVE, unexpired and unrevoked, and bound to the path/resource org. Every required permission is in `scopes` **and** in the issuer's permissions at issuance (ceiling, 6B §16.4, FAR #9). | Revoked/expired/invalid → 401; scope/ceiling → 403; foreign org → 404 |
| `C-KEY-SM` | `C-KEY` holds, **and** the sensitive scope (`recording:access_media` / `transcript:access_content`) is **explicitly** in `scopes`. It is never implied by `recording:read`, `transcript:read` or `call:read`. The issuer must also have held it (OWNER/ADMIN, or a custom role holding it). | 403 `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` |
| `C-CR` | The custom role's explicit `role_permissions` contain every required permission (AND). No inheritance. | 403 |
| `C-SUBSET` | AAM-OD-02 A (6B §21.31 L1922/§21.33 L1970 as amended). Applies to AMI-6B-029/031, which create or edit **custom** roles (`is_system = false`) only. `target_role.is_system == false → permissions(target_role) ⊆ effective_permissions(actor)`: every requested permission (create) or every permission of the resulting set (update) is in the actor's own effective set. OWNER is unrestricted within the 70-permission tenant catalogue. `analytics_platform:read` is never grantable. A custom role never confers PLATFORM_ADMIN. | 403 `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` |
| `C-ATTACH` | AAM-OD-02 A (6C §8.4 L326/§15.16 L849 as amended). Applies to AMI-6C-010 (assign) and AMI-6C-016 (invite). **System target** (`is_system = true`): the frozen hierarchy is unchanged — ADMIN may assign or invite `ADMIN`, `MEMBER`, `BILLING_ADMIN` or `VIEWER`; `OWNER` is never a valid target (ownership moves only through 6C §8.5). **Custom target** (`is_system = false`): the `C-SUBSET` rule. For a key (6C-016), the key's effective set (scopes ∩ issuer) is the actor's set. | Custom-role subset violation → 403 `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`; OWNER target → 409 `ROLE_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` (6C-010, 6C §8.4) or the 6C §15.16 `role_id` validation rejection (6C-016) |
| `C-BG-R-GENERAL` | PA token. `X-Break-Glass-Grant` is present and passes all six checks of 6B §18.3a (exists, not expired, not released, org = path org, admin = caller, session = caller session). `grant.purposes` contains `SUPPORT_GENERAL`. Access is audited. | No header, a grant that fails a check, or a grant that lacks the purpose → 403 `BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` (FAR L261 #10; AAM-F-07); Redis unavailable → 503 `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` |
| `C-BG-R-BILLING` | As `C-BG-R-GENERAL`, with purpose `SUPPORT_BILLING` (6K-owned `GET /api/v1/billing/*` except quotas) | as above |
| `C-BG-R-QUOTA` | As `C-BG-R-GENERAL`, with purpose `SUPPORT_QUOTA` (`GET /api/v1/billing/quotas`) | as above |
| `C-BG-R-LIFECYCLE` | As `C-BG-R-GENERAL`, with purpose `SUPPORT_ORG_LIFECYCLE` (`GET /api/v1/organizations/{organization_id}`) | as above |
| `C-BG-SM` | PA token plus a grant passing the six checks. The purpose `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS` is hardcoded by the guarded function. Also requires `session_user='app_platform_admin'` + `fn_break_glass_check` and an in-transaction audit record (107_5B5). | Missing/invalid grant or wrong purpose → 403 `BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` |
| `C-AUTHOR` | The caller is the note's author, or the note is AI-generated (DEP-6G-02). | 403 |
| `C-TARGET` | The principal holds `contact:write` when the target is a contact, and `deal:write` when it is a deal. | 403 |
| `C-JOB` | AAM-OD-04 C (6A §18.3 L488 as amended; registry §7.3b). (1) Authenticate. (2) Establish tenant context (session/membership, principal-type eligibility). (3) Resolve the job server-side from the path `job_id`. (4) Nonexistent job → 404. (5) Job organization ≠ caller organization → 404. (6) Resolve the job's canonical type from server-stored data. (7) Load that type's registry entry; a type with no entry is denied (no generic fallback). (8) Allow if the entry supports an initiator **and** the caller matches the server-recorded initiator exactly on principal type **and** id, **or** the caller holds the entry's server-defined `view_permission` (for a key: via `C-KEY`; for a custom role: via `C-CR`). (9) Otherwise 403. Type, tenant, initiator and permission are never taken from or inferred from client input, and no UUID is matched without its principal type. No V1 registry entry supports an initiator: `campaign.csv_import_jobs.created_by` (028_5E) is a bare UUID with no principal type and is not an initiator. PLATFORM_ADMIN never satisfies `C-JOB` and never acquires tenant membership, a tenant role, a tenant `view_permission` such as `integration:read` or the initiator shortcut. The two branches differ only in how the tenant scope is obtained: a **tenant principal** already has one from its authenticated session or key, resolves the job inside it and is authorized by steps (1)–(9) above; a **`PLATFORM_ADMIN`** has none, so the server first validates its `SUPPORT_GENERAL` break-glass grant far enough to read the server-stored `grant.organization_id`, uses that one organization as the candidate lookup namespace, resolves the job inside it (missing → 404), re-checks `resource.organization_id == grant.organization_id`, completes the break-glass authorization, and only then establishes a tenant context and exposes status or result — `C-BG-R-GENERAL`, read-only and audited (6A L488 rule (8a); §7.3d). Existence, organization, type and lifecycle state come only from the registry source (a row, or for connection sync the lifecycle-bound Redis record, §7.3c); a result-backend state is read only after steps 1–8 pass and never proves, creates or resurrects a job (6A L488 rule 9). | Missing or foreign-org job → 404 `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` (connection sync: missing/expired Redis record → 404; Celery `PENDING` with no record → 404; past `result_available_until` → 404); no `view_permission` → 403 `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`; connection-sync Redis unavailable → 503 `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; connection-sync result lost before `result_available_until` → 503 `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` + integrity alert |
| `C-PREAUTH` | A valid, unexpired, unused single-use token of the purpose this route consumes (`EMAIL_VERIFICATION`, `PASSWORD_RESET`, `INVITATION`), or the enumeration-safe unauthenticated path where the row allows it. | owning contract (6B) |
| `C-REFRESH` | A valid, unrevoked refresh token in its rotation family. | 401 |
| `C-RT-MFA` | An `mfa_challenge` token, accepted only here. | 401 |
| `C-RT-LCT` | A `login_continuation` token, accepted only here. | 401 |
| `C-INT` | Internal JWT (`token_use=internal`), an allow-listed `service_id`, and `on_behalf_of_organization_id` = path org. | Wrong token type → 401; org mismatch/foreign → 404 |
| `C-INT-PA` | Internal JWT, allow-listed `service_id`, and issuer-asserted `actor_type=PLATFORM_ADMIN` (6D §28.36/§28.37). This is not a permission string. | 401 |
| `C-PROV` | Provider-native signature verified per adapter; tenant resolved after verification. | owning contract (no code invented) |
| `C-PROV-WS` | Provider media stream correlated to a call created by the call-setup flow (6D L405). | owning contract |
| `C-STATE` | OAuth `state` redeemed exactly once and bound to the initiating org/user. | owning contract |

### 7.3a Platform-admin outcomes on tenant routes (the distinctions preserved)

| Situation | Result | Source |
|---|---|---|
| PA token, **no** `X-Break-Glass-Grant` header, read-oriented tenant route (break-glass required) | `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` | FAR L261 code #10 ("missing header" is one of the collapsed failures); 6B L911, L960, L2083. The lower-precedence rows 6B §18.3a L967 and §31 L2344 (`AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`) and 6C §17 L997 (bare "403") are stale and superseded by FAR precedence (AAM-F-07, RESOLVED BY PRECEDENCE). |
| PA token, header present, grant fails any of the six checks (not found, expired, released, wrong org, wrong admin, wrong session), read-oriented route | `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` | 6B L925–933, L960–966, §31 L2346–2349; FAR code #10 |
| PA token, valid grant, `purposes` lacks the route's mapped purpose (§9.2) | `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` | 6B §18.3b item 2 (L983, as amended by AAM-OD-03 C) |
| PA token, valid grant containing the mapped purpose, read-oriented route | authorized (audited) | 6B §31 L2344; 6C §17 L998; AAM-OD-03 C |
| PA token, any header state (none, invalid or valid), tenant **write** route | `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` | 6B §18.4 L1006 ("only read-oriented break-glass access … in scope"); §31 L2343. Break-glass does not apply, so the missing-header rule above does not either. |
| PA token, grant only `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS` and/or `SUPPORT_SECURITY_INCIDENT`, generic tenant read | `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` | 6B L983 (as amended) |
| PA token on AMI-6D-011/014 (tenant media routes), with any grant | `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` | 6D §25; 6M §27.2 (PA media only via 6M-008/009) |
| AMI-6M-008/009, missing or invalid grant, or purpose ≠ `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS` | `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` | 6B L911; 6M §13/§27.2; FAR #10, #15 |
| Redis unavailable during grant validation | `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, access denied | 6B L920, L959 |

### 7.3c Connection-sync authorization-record lifecycle (AAM-F-12; normative source 6J §15.3 L581, 6A §18.3 L488)

The `integrations.connection_sync` record (`jobauth:{organization_id}:{job_id}`, Redis) is bound to the job's lifecycle, not to a TTL fixed at submission. All durations are server configuration: `R` result-backend retention (`result_expires`), `Q` maximum queue age, `T` hard time limit, `L` running lease, `H` heartbeat (`H ≤ L/3`), `I` reconciler interval (`I ≤ L/3`), `S` safety margin (`S ≥ I` + clock tolerance), `G` enqueue-confirmation grace, `X` result-recovery grace (`X ≥ 2I`; `R > L + S + X`). `terminal_source` is `WORKER`, `RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY` or `RECONCILER`.

| Internal state | Entered when | Key expiry | Public status | Cleanup if stuck |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `SUBMITTED` | Server generates `job_id`, writes the record (`SET NX`) before enqueue and before the `202` | `submitted_at + Q + L + S` | `PENDING` | Enqueue failure → synchronous delete + `503`, no `202`; reconciler deletes after `G` |
| `QUEUED` | Enqueue confirmed (Celery `expires = submitted_at + Q`) | unchanged — already outlives the latest possible start | `PENDING` | Past `submitted_at + Q` → reconciler `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` |
| `RUNNING` | Worker compare-and-set from `SUBMITTED`/`QUEUED`; never creates a record (failed compare-and-set → no work) | renewed to `now + L + S` every `H` (compare-and-set on `RUNNING`), until `started_at + T` | `RUNNING` | Lease expired or past `started_at + T + L` → reconciler **queries the result backend first**: stored result → recovered (see below); lookup succeeds, no result → `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` (worker lost); backend unavailable → no write, stays `RUNNING`, retried, expiry extended to at most `started_at + T + L + S + X`, then unclassified expiry + alert |
| `TERMINAL` | Worker-side terminalizer (`task_postrun`, after Celery persisted the result; never the task body) compare-and-set with the stored status and `terminal_at` = `date_done` (`WORKER`); reconciler recovery of a stored result with its `date_done` (`RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`); or reconciler fallback at reconciliation time (`RECONCILER`) | `result_available_until + S`, with `result_available_until = terminal_at + R` | `terminal_status` (`SUCCEEDED`/`FAILED`) | Final; later writes ignored; finite expiry removes it |
| `EXPIRED` | Past `result_available_until`, or record absent | — | none: `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` | — (record lifetime ≤ `Q + T + L + X + R + 2S`) |

Lookup order for `GET /api/v1/jobs/{job_id}`: authenticate → resolve the Redis record (Redis unavailable → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; missing or expired → `404`) → organization match (foreign → `404`) → `integration:read` (tenant principal; API key via `C-KEY`; else `403`), or PLATFORM_ADMIN via `C-BG-R-GENERAL` only → only then the Celery result backend. A Celery `PENDING` never proves existence (no record → `404`, never recreated); a worker-terminal result missing before `result_available_until`, or an unreachable backend → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` plus an integrity alert, never a false `PENDING`. No migration, no table, no Celery extended metadata as the authorization source.

Stale-`RUNNING` reconciliation precedence (6J L581 (3e)): a stored backend terminal result always wins over the worker-lost fallback.

| Result-backend lookup for a stale `RUNNING` record | Reconciler action | `terminal_status` | `terminal_source` | `terminal_at` | `result_available_until` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Terminal result stored (`SUCCESS`/`FAILURE`) | compare-and-set `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL` | stored outcome (`SUCCEEDED`/`FAILED`) | `RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY` | stored `date_done` (never the reconciliation time) | `date_done + R` |
| Lookup succeeds, no terminal result | compare-and-set `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL` | `FAILED` (worker lost) | `RECONCILER` | reconciliation time | reconciliation time `+ R` |
| Backend unavailable / lookup fails | no terminal write, no guess; alert; retry next run | — (stays `RUNNING`) | — | — | — (expiry ≤ `started_at + T + L + S + X`, then unclassified expiry → `404` + integrity alert) |

Every terminal write is a compare-and-set from a non-terminal state, so a late worker, terminalizer or reconciler write never regresses or replaces `TERMINAL`; the read path never writes the record.

Permitted and rejected transitions (every write is a compare-and-set from a named predecessor state; the sender’s post-publication confirmation attempt is a confirmation only — it happens after the broker publication has succeeded, is not a transactional prerequisite of acceptance, and never revokes the task, deletes the record or regresses a state, and a definitive broker-publication failure remains a distinct, compensating failure):

| Transition | Permitted | Writer and rule |
|---|---|---|
| `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` | yes | API sender, as its post-publication confirmation: one compare-and-set from `SUBMITTED` after the broker publication has been confirmed; the record keeps its queued expiry `submitted_at + Q + L + S` |
| `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` | yes | worker, when it starts before the confirmation lands: compare-and-set from `SUBMITTED` before any external side effect |
| `QUEUED` → `RUNNING` | yes | worker: compare-and-set from `QUEUED`, lease `now + L + S`, renewed every `H` |
| `QUEUED` → `TERMINAL` | yes | **reconciler only** — the conditional not-started path: a queued record past `submitted_at + Q` is moved to `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` (never started) with `terminal_source = RECONCILER` (6J §15.3 L581 clause (3e)). The post-persistence terminalizer of clause (3d) writes only `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL`, after the task result is stored |
| `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL` | yes | post-persistence terminalizer (`task_postrun`), or the reconciler by result recovery (`RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`) or worker-lost classification |
| `RUNNING` → `QUEUED` | no (rejected) | a late sender confirmation never regresses a started job: the failed compare-and-set is abandoned — the sender never revokes the task, deletes the record or answers `503`; the `202` stands |
| `TERMINAL` → `QUEUED` | no (rejected) | `TERMINAL` is final; an early completion is a successful submission and the confirmation is abandoned |
| `TERMINAL` → `RUNNING` | no (rejected) | `TERMINAL` is final against every late writer, worker or reconciler |
| `RUNNING` → `SUBMITTED` | no (rejected) | the lifecycle is monotonic; no writer rewinds a record |
| `QUEUED` → `SUBMITTED` | no (rejected) | the lifecycle is monotonic; no writer recreates or rewinds a record |

Lifecycle invariants, parsed from the live sources by the assembler (gate ZB); the independent validator and Sweep D re-derive them:

| ID | Invariant | Source (live) | Phrases required | Phrases forbidden | Holds |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| LC-01 | Lifecycle-bound record, never a submission-relative TTL | 6J §15.3 L581 | "**lifecycle-bound, never submission-relative**"; "measured from the actual terminal time, not from submission" | "`result_available_until = submitted_at"; "TTL measured from submission" | yes |
| LC-02 | Server-generated id; record created (`SET NX`) before the `202` and before enqueue | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the server generates the `job_id` (the Celery task ID); the client never chooses or alters it"; "before returning `202`, and before the task is enqueued, the server writes a Redis authorization record"; "created with `SET NX` and never overwritten by a new submission" | — | yes |
| LC-03 | Enqueue failure compensates: record deleted, `503`, never `202` | 6J §15.3 L581 | "if the write fails, no task is enqueued and no `202` is returned"; "the server deletes the `SUBMITTED` record synchronously and returns `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `202`, and no task executes" | — | yes |
| LC-04 | Queued durability: expiry outlives the latest possible start; Celery `expires` bounds the queue | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the record is written in `SUBMITTED` with key expiry `submitted_at + Q + L + S`"; "the task is published to the broker with Celery `expires = submitted_at + Q`, so no worker can start it later"; "the key expiry written at submission already outlives the latest possible start" | — | yes |
| LC-05 | Running lease renewal by the worker, bounded by the hard time limit | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the worker renews `lease_expires_at` and the key expiry to `now + L + S` every `H`"; "stops renewing at `started_at + T`"; "each renewal is a compare-and-set on `RUNNING`" | — | yes |
| LC-06 | Worker never creates or recreates a record (CAS transitions only) | 6J §15.3 L581 | "it never creates or recreates a record: receipt of the Celery message alone never authorizes work"; "moving the record by compare-and-set from an allowed predecessor" | — | yes |
| LC-07 | Terminal deadline = actual terminal time + result retention, computed at terminal | 6J §15.3 L581 | "only after the task result is stored in the result backend, the worker moves the record from `RUNNING` to `TERMINAL` by compare-and-set"; "`terminal_at` = the backend's completion time (`date_done`)"; "`result_available_until = terminal_at + R`"; "key expiry `result_available_until + S`"; "the record outlives the whole result-retention window measured from the actual terminal time, not from submission" | "`terminal_at` = `submitted_at`"; "deadline from submission time" | yes |
| LC-08 | Terminal is final; states never move backwards | 6J §15.3 L581 | "`TERMINAL` is final and any later write is ignored"; "no state ever moves backwards" | — | yes |
| LC-09 | Bounded orphan/stale cleanup by a server reconciler, with finite expiry as backstop | 6J §15.3 L581 | "a periodic server reconciler (APScheduler, the stale-reaper pattern of 3B and 3C) deletes a `SUBMITTED` record older than `G`"; "moves a `QUEUED` record past `submitted_at + Q` to `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` (not started) with `terminal_source = RECONCILER`"; "if the reconciler is itself down, the finite key expiry still removes the record, so no record is immortal"; "lifetime at most `Q + T + L + X + R + 2S`"; "no job stays `PENDING` forever" | "the record is retained indefinitely" | yes |
| LC-10 | Lookup order: record, organization and permission before any Celery read | 6J §15.3 L581 | "consults the Celery result backend only after authentication, record resolution, the organization match and the permission check" | — | yes |
| LC-11 | Public status projected from the record; no new public status | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the public status comes from the record"; "`SUBMITTED` and `QUEUED` → `PENDING`, `RUNNING` → `RUNNING`, `TERMINAL` → its `terminal_status`"; "no new public status is introduced" | — | yes |
| LC-12 | Celery `PENDING` never proves existence; no record → 404; never resurrected | 6J §15.3 L581 | "Celery reports `PENDING` for any unknown or expired task id, so a Celery `PENDING` never proves that a job exists"; "with no record the answer is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, never `PENDING`"; "no record is ever recreated from Celery" | — | yes |
| LC-13 | Past `result_available_until` → 404, never `PENDING` | 6J §15.3 L581 | "once `result_available_until` has passed (within the clock tolerance) the job is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`" | — | yes |
| LC-14 | Result lost before the deadline, or backend unreachable → 503 + integrity alert, never a false `PENDING` | 6J §15.3 L581 | "a worker-written `TERMINAL` result that the backend no longer returns before `result_available_until`, or a result backend that is unreachable, is answered with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` and raises an integrity alert, never a false `PENDING`" | — | yes |
| LC-15 | Redis unavailable → 503, never 404 | 6J §15.3 L581 | "Redis unavailability is never treated as not-found: the request fails closed with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`"; "a missing or expired record is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, identical to a nonexistent or cross-tenant job" | — | yes |
| LC-16 | No migration, no table, no Celery-metadata authorization source | 6J §15.3 L581 | "no migration and no `IntegrationSyncJob` table is added in V1" | "migration 113"; "result_extended" | yes |
| LC-17 | Registry source (iv) is the lifecycle; Celery supplies terminal detail only after authorization | 6A §18.3 L488 | "whose server-controlled lifecycle (`SUBMITTED`/`QUEUED` → `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL`, kept until `result_available_until` = actual terminal time + the result-backend retention, then expired) alone establishes the job's existence and status"; "the Celery result backend supplying only terminal detail after authorization" | — | yes |
| LC-18 | Generic rule (9): existence only from the registry source; unavailable → 503 | 6A §18.3 L488 | "a job exists only while its registry source (row or server-written record) exists"; "a result-backend state such as Celery `PENDING` never proves, creates or resurrects a job"; "an unavailable source fails closed with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `404` and never a guessed status" | — | yes |
| LC-19 | 6A §18.3 L486 `PREPARING` wording clarified: no `/jobs` projection, exactly four job types | 6A §18.3 L486 | "campaign `PREPARING` materialization is **not** projected through `/api/v1/jobs/{job_id}`"; "mints no `job_id` and is not a job type"; "the V1 job types are exactly the four registry entries" | — | yes |
| LC-20 | Stale `RUNNING`: the reconciler queries the result backend first, before any worker-lost classification | 6J §15.3 L581 | "a `RUNNING` record whose lease has expired, or that is past `started_at + T + L`, is stale"; "the reconciler **queries the result backend first**, by the record's server-generated task id, before any worker-lost classification" | "classifies the worker as lost before querying" | yes |
| LC-21 | Stored terminal result wins: recovered with its stored status and `date_done` (`RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`) | 6J §15.3 L581 | "if the backend holds a terminal result for that task (`SUCCESS` or `FAILURE`), the reconciler moves the record from `RUNNING` to `TERMINAL` by compare-and-set with the stored outcome as `terminal_status`"; "`terminal_source = RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`"; "`terminal_at` = the stored result's `date_done`, never the reconciliation time"; "`result_available_until = terminal_at + R`"; "so a job the backend stored as succeeded is never reported as worker-lost `FAILED`" | "`terminal_source = RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`, `terminal_at` = the reconciliation time" | yes |
| LC-22 | Worker-lost `FAILED` only when the backend lookup succeeds with no terminal result | 6J §15.3 L581 | "only when the lookup succeeds and the backend holds no terminal result for that task does the reconciler move the record to `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` (worker lost)" | "if the lookup fails the reconciler moves the record to `TERMINAL`/`FAILED`" | yes |
| LC-23 | Result backend unavailable: no terminal write, no guessed outcome, bounded retry, then unclassified expiry | 6J §15.3 L581 | "if the lookup fails or the backend is unreachable, the reconciler writes no terminal state and guesses no outcome"; "the record stays `RUNNING`, a dependency alert is raised and the reconciler retries on its next run"; "extending the key expiry to at most `now + I + S` and never beyond `started_at + T + L + S + X`"; "if the backend is still unavailable at that bound the record expires unclassified"; "an integrity alert is raised" | "guesses a `FAILED` outcome" | yes |
| LC-24 | Worker terminal write by a terminalizer after result persistence, never by the task body | 6J §15.3 L581 | "this write is made by a worker-side terminalizer that runs after Celery has persisted the task's terminal state (a `task_postrun` signal handler), never by the task body"; "the terminalizer reads the stored result back by the server-generated task id"; "if the stored result cannot be read back, or no handler runs" | — | yes |
| LC-25 | Terminal finality against late writes; a stored result always beats the worker-lost fallback; reads never write | 6J §15.3 L581 | "every terminal write is a compare-and-set from a non-terminal state, so a late worker, terminalizer or reconciler write never regresses or replaces a `TERMINAL` record"; "a stored backend terminal result always takes precedence over the worker-lost fallback"; "the read path never writes the record, never creates a job and never moves a state backwards"; "a `TERMINAL` record with `terminal_source = RECONCILER` (not started or worker lost) is answered as `FAILED` from the record alone, and a backend result stored after it never replaces it" | — | yes |
| LC-26 | Retention covers recovery (`R > L + S + X`); lifetime bound includes the recovery grace | 6J §15.3 L581 | "`X` = the result-recovery grace (`X ≥ 2I`, and `R > L + S + X`, so a stored result is still retained when the reconciler looks for it)"; "lifetime at most `Q + T + L + X + R + 2S`" | — | yes |
| LC-27 | Monotonic lifecycle: the permitted transitions are enumerated exhaustively | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the lifecycle is **monotonic** — the only permitted transitions are `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` (the sender's post-publication confirmation), `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` and `QUEUED` → `RUNNING` (a worker starting work), `QUEUED` → `TERMINAL` (never started) and `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL` (terminalizer or reconciler)" | — | yes |
| LC-28 | Backward transitions are enumerated and rejected | 6J §15.3 L581 | "so `RUNNING` → `QUEUED`, `TERMINAL` → `QUEUED`, `TERMINAL` → `RUNNING`, `RUNNING` → `SUBMITTED` and `QUEUED` → `SUBMITTED` are rejected and no state ever moves backwards" | "`RUNNING` → `QUEUED` is permitted"; "the sender resets the record to `QUEUED`" | yes |
| LC-29 | Every transition is a compare-and-set from a named predecessor; nothing is written unconditionally | 6J §15.3 L581 | "every transition is a compare-and-set from a named predecessor state and no actor ever writes a state unconditionally" | "writes the state unconditionally" | yes |
| LC-30 | Definitive publication failure stays distinct from a lost or unobservable confirmation | 6J §15.3 L581 | "if the publication **definitively fails** — the broker rejects it or does not accept it — the server deletes the `SUBMITTED` record synchronously and returns `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`"; "a definitive publication failure (record deleted, `503`, nothing executes) stays distinct from a confirmation that merely lost the race or could not be observed (`202`, record preserved)"; "a client is never told to retry a sync that was in fact accepted" | — | yes |
| LC-31 | The post-publication confirmation is a monotonic confirmation, never authority over a progressed job | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the sender attempts one compare-and-set `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED`, which is a monotonic *confirmation* and not authority over a job that has already progressed"; "the sender never revokes, deletes or overwrites a record because its own compare-and-set lost"; "it interprets the outcome by reading the authoritative record" | — | yes |
| LC-32 | Cases (i)/(ii): confirmation succeeds, or is idempotently already `QUEUED` — `202`, no rewrite | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(i) the compare-and-set succeeds, the state is `QUEUED` and the server returns `202`"; "(ii) the record is already `QUEUED` (a retried or duplicate confirmation), which is idempotently successful, is not rewritten, and returns `202`" | — | yes |
| LC-33 | Case (iii): the worker wins the race — `202`, `RUNNING` preserved, no revoke and no false `503` | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(iii) the record is already `RUNNING`, i.e. a fast worker legitimately moved `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` before the confirmation — the worker winning that race is a **successful submission**, `202`, and `RUNNING` is preserved: no revoke, no delete, no regression to `QUEUED` and no false `503`" | "the sender revokes the task because the record is `RUNNING`" | yes |
| LC-34 | Case (iv): an early `TERMINAL` is a successful submission and is never regressed to `QUEUED` | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(iv) the record is already `TERMINAL`, i.e. the task also finished before the confirmation — this is likewise a successful submission, `202`, and the terminal result is preserved unchanged and never regressed to `QUEUED`" | "the sender overwrites the terminal result with `QUEUED`" | yes |
| LC-35 | Case (v): transient contention is retried within the request; nothing is deleted or revoked | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(v) the record is still `SUBMITTED` and the compare-and-set lost to transient contention, in which case the sender retries the read-and-compare-and-set sequence a bounded number of times within the request and never deletes or revokes the job merely because one attempt failed" | — | yes |
| LC-36 | Case (vi): an unwritable confirmation is never evidence of publication failure | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(vi) Redis is unavailable for the confirmation although the prewrite and the publication both succeeded — a confirmation that cannot be written is never evidence that publication failed, so the server deletes and revokes nothing, keeps the accepted submission"; "leaves the worker free to move `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` and the reconciler free to handle a record left in `SUBMITTED` under (3e)"; "answers `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` for as long as Redis itself is unavailable" | "deletes the record when the confirmation cannot be written" | yes |
| LC-37 | Case (vii): an absent record after publication fails closed; it is never recreated from Celery | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(vii) the record is absent while Redis is reachable, which is an integrity failure after publication: the server never recreates it from Celery, raises an integrity alert, makes a best-effort revoke of the published task and returns the dependency-failure response rather than claiming success"; "the worker rule of (3c) already prevents work without a record" | — | yes |
| LC-38 | Case (viii): any other or corrupt state fails closed, with no overwrite and no invented data | 6J §15.3 L581 | "(viii) any other or corrupt state fails closed with an integrity alert, with no overwrite, no delete and no manufactured lifecycle data" | — | yes |
| LC-39 | The confirmation is never a transactional prerequisite for accepting the job | 6J §15.3 L581 | "the post-publication confirmation is therefore never a transactional prerequisite for accepting the job" | — | yes |
| LC-40 | The worker establishes `RUNNING` by CAS before any side effect, and does no work without a valid record | 6J §15.3 L581 | "before any provider-visible or otherwise external side effect the worker must itself establish `RUNNING`"; "from `SUBMITTED` when it starts before the sender's confirmation, or from `QUEUED`"; "if the record is absent, expired, belongs to another job, is in a state from which `RUNNING` is not reachable, or cannot be safely consulted because Redis is unavailable, the worker does no work, invents no authorization metadata and releases the `integrationsync:{connection_id}` lock"; "it does the same when that compare-and-set fails because the record is no longer `SUBMITTED` or `QUEUED`" | — | yes |
| LC-41 | Case (vi) residual: a record left in `SUBMITTED` is bounded by the enqueue-confirmation grace `G` | 6J §15.3 L581 | "deletes a `SUBMITTED` record older than `G`"; "`G` = the enqueue-confirmation grace" | — | yes |

### 7.3d Generic-job resolution for a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` (AMI-6A-001; normative source 6A §18.3 L488 rule (8a))

A tenant principal reaches a job through the tenant context it already has; a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` has none. Where the registered backing source is keyed by organization — `integrations.connection_sync`, whose authorization record is `jobauth:{organization_id}:{job_id}` (§7.3c) — the organization would be needed in order to find the record that carries the organization. 6A L488 rule (8a) resolves that circularity by taking the missing organization from the server’s own stored break-glass grant, never from the caller. It adds no route, permission, public error code, table, index or migration, and gives a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` nothing beyond the purpose-scoped, read-only, audited break-glass read it already held: the AMI-6A-001 platform-admin cell stays `CONDITIONAL C-BG-R-GENERAL`.

The resolution order this matrix applies:

1. The `PLATFORM_ADMIN` authenticates normally (platform-admin token; no tenant session, no membership).
2. The request must carry the break-glass grant reference of the existing 6B §18.3a contract (`X-Break-Glass-Grant`). No reference, or one the server cannot resolve, is `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` — never `404`, and no resource lookup is attempted.
3. The grant is loaded **server-side** from `organization.break_glass_grants` through the 6B §18.3a validation path. Nothing about it is taken from the request beyond the reference itself.
4. Every grant check that does not depend on the resource runs first: the grant exists, its `status` is `ACTIVE` and not `RELEASED`, it is not past `expires_at`, its `admin_user_id` is the calling admin, its `session_id` is the calling session, and its purpose is the `SUPPORT_GENERAL` this route requires (AAM-OD-03 C). Passing these does **not** yet authorize the read.
5. Only then does the server read `candidate_organization_id = grant.organization_id` from the stored grant row. This is a **lookup namespace, not a permission**. It is never taken from, defaulted from or overridden by a query parameter, a request body, any header other than the grant reference, a route variable, client-submitted job metadata or caller-supplied Celery task metadata; a client-supplied organization identifier is ignored entirely (§8, AAM-F-07).
6. The job is resolved from its registered backing source **inside that one organization only** — for `integrations.connection_sync` exactly the key `jobauth:{grant.organization_id}:{job_id}`, and for a table-backed entry exactly `WHERE organization_id = grant.organization_id AND job_id = :job_id`.
7. No record under that namespace is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, indistinguishable from a job that never existed. A job held in another organization while the grant targets this one is therefore `404`, and the other organization is never searched, so the server does not learn the job exists elsewhere.
8. A resolved record yields its organization from the **server-stored** record itself, never from the request.
9. The mandatory break-glass binding of rule (8) still runs on that value: `resource.organization_id == grant.organization_id`. The candidate namespace narrows the search; it never substitutes for this check. A record whose embedded organization differs from the grant organization fails closed — no tenant context, no job state, no existence signal — and raises the existing integrity/security alert path of that source contract; no new public error code is minted for that corrupt-data case.
10. Only after the break-glass authorization is **complete** may the server establish a tenant context for the request, expose the job’s lifecycle state, consult the Celery result backend for terminal detail (6A L488 rule (9)), and emit the 6B §18.3a break-glass audit event, which records the grant id and the resolved target organization.
11. The `PLATFORM_ADMIN` acquires no tenant role membership, no tenant `C-JOB`, no tenant `integration:read` and no initiator shortcut at any point; the read stays read-only.
12. The read is audited under the existing break-glass audit contract — the same contract, extended by nothing.

Forbidden; an implementation doing any of these is non-conformant:

- `SCAN jobauth:*:{job_id}` or any other pattern scan across Redis key namespaces;
- iterating organizations, or querying each tenant’s job table, until a `job_id` matches;
- an undocumented global reverse index from `job_id` to organization (no such index exists, and none is introduced);
- automatically retrying the lookup against a second or subsequent break-glass grant — one request and one grant yield exactly one candidate namespace;
- taking the candidate organization from client input in any form.

Error precedence (AAM-F-07 is preserved, not regressed):

- missing or unresolvable break-glass reference → `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID`, before any lookup, never `404`;
- invalid, expired, released, revoked, wrong-purpose or actor/session-mismatched grant → the canonical 6B §18.3a break-glass failure, and that grant is never used as a resource-location namespace;
- valid `SUPPORT_GENERAL` grant, no matching job in the grant’s organization → `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`;
- valid grant for organization B while the job exists only in organization A → `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, with no cross-tenant probe and no existence signal;
- the backing source unavailable (Redis down) → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `404` and never a guessed state.

The tenant branch is unchanged: a system-role user, a custom-role user or an API key is resolved inside its own authenticated organization, the job is looked up only there, and `C-JOB` (§7.3, with `C-KEY` or `C-CR` where they apply) decides the read against the registry entry’s `view_permission`.

### 7.4 AUTH / SELF rows (no tenant permission)

- **Pre-authentication rows** (AMI-6B-001/002/008/012) are `ALLOW (ANON)`.
- **Restricted-token rows** are `CONDITIONAL` on the matching restricted token.
- **Self-scoped access-token rows** are `ALLOW` for every authenticated user regardless of role, because the row evaluates only the caller's own identity and sessions (see the Guards column). They are:
  - `N/A` for PA (self identity, not PA authority);
  - `DENY 403` for API keys, unless the row lists API key (AMI-6B-018, 6B-027). This includes AMI-6C-038/039/040 (`/users/me`) by AAM-OD-01 A;
  - `DENY 401` for internal tokens and anonymous callers.
- **AMI-6C-001** (create organization) is authentication-only (6C §17: authenticated user with no org → 201).
- **AMI-6C-014** (leave organization) is membership-self with the last-OWNER guard. The 409 is state, not RBAC.

### 7.5 API-key eligibility

**D1: key-ineligible routes (API key → `DENY 403`; computed count 67 rows, listed from §13):** AMI-6B-004, AMI-6B-007, AMI-6B-010, AMI-6B-014, AMI-6B-015, AMI-6B-016, AMI-6B-017, AMI-6B-019, AMI-6B-020, AMI-6B-021, AMI-6B-022, AMI-6B-023, AMI-6B-026, AMI-6B-029, AMI-6B-031, AMI-6B-032, AMI-6B-033, AMI-6C-001, AMI-6C-003, AMI-6C-004, AMI-6C-005, AMI-6C-006, AMI-6C-007, AMI-6C-010, AMI-6C-014, AMI-6C-015, AMI-6C-038, AMI-6C-039, AMI-6C-040, AMI-6D-012, AMI-6D-019, AMI-6E-005, AMI-6E-006, AMI-6G-011, AMI-6G-012, AMI-6G-034, AMI-6G-035, AMI-6G-036, AMI-6G-070, AMI-6G-072, AMI-6G-073, AMI-6G-078, AMI-6H-007, AMI-6H-009, AMI-6I-012, AMI-6K-001, AMI-6K-002, AMI-6K-003, AMI-6K-004, AMI-6K-005, AMI-6K-006, AMI-6K-007, AMI-6K-008, AMI-6K-009, AMI-6K-010, AMI-6K-011, AMI-6K-012, AMI-6K-013, AMI-6K-014, AMI-6K-015, AMI-6K-016, AMI-6K-017, AMI-6K-018, AMI-6K-019, AMI-6K-020, AMI-6K-021, AMI-6K-022. Source: the owning contract's Key column or auth cell, and AAM-OD-01 A for AMI-6C-038/039/040. Status 403 per FAR code #9 (AAM-F-04).

**D2: 6C rows whose key eligibility is "NOT SPECIFIED IN ROW"** fall back to 6B §17.3 (keys accepted on tenant routes, subject to the ceiling) → `C-KEY` (AAM-I-01). The `/users/me` rows are excluded because AAM-OD-01 A decides them.

**Platform-admin routes:** key → `DENY 403 TPF` unconditionally (FAR #13). **Internal routes:** key → `DENY 401` (FAR #10). **Callbacks:** `DENY SIG`.

### 7.6 Guards and quotas are not authorization

- The Guards column lists guards: last-OWNER, own-session, author, identity-bound OWNER, plugin visibility and `is_system` protection. They can only narrow access. The custom-role subset rule is expressed as `C-SUBSET` / `C-ATTACH` because it is a condition on the requested content, not on the route.
- Quota, capacity, rate-limit and plan-entitlement checks are **never** authorization. Where the AMI notes mention quota or capacity, the Guards column says "quota/capacity (not authorization)". Quota exhaustion (429/402-class per the owning contract) never changes an `ALLOW`/`DENY` cell (AAM-ADV-19).

---

## 8. Tenant Isolation and Cross-Tenant Behaviour

1. Tenant identity is derived by the server only, from the verified access-token claim or the API-key row (6B principle 2; 6A §23.2). A client-supplied `organization_id` never selects the tenant.
2. A request for a resource in another organization, by any tenant role, custom role or API key, returns **`404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`**. This is indistinguishable from non-existence (6A §23 L595; 6B §9.2; 6C §17 "ADMIN cross-org → 404"). A 403 for a foreign-tenant resource is a defect (AAM-ADV-15).
3. The permission gate is evaluated only after the tenant gate, so a foreign-tenant probe cannot learn whether the caller would have held the permission.
4. RLS on every tenant table is defence in depth under the application check, not a replacement for it.
5. Cross-tenant reach exists only for:
   - (a) PLATFORM_ADMIN on `/api/v1/platform-admin/*` (§9);
   - (b) PLATFORM_ADMIN on read-oriented tenant routes with a valid, purpose-matching break-glass grant bound to that org (`C-BG-R-*`, §9.2);
   - (c) the two internal PA/support lookups (AMI-6D-020, AMI-6E-017; `C-INT-PA`).

   Each is recorded row by row in §13's Cross-tenant column.
6. API keys are org-bound: a key from org A used on org B's resource → 404 (6L test table L1205–1222).

---

## 9. Platform Admin and Break-Glass

### 9.1 Rules

1. **Disjoint from tenant RBAC** (FAR #12). PLATFORM_ADMIN is not a tenant role, and `ADMIN` is not a platform admin. PA holds no tenant permission and never satisfies a tenant permission check by itself.
2. **Platform-admin routes** (`/api/v1/platform-admin/*`; computed count 42: AMI-6B-034/035/036 and all 39 AMI-6M rows).
   - Every tenant role, custom role and API key → `DENY 403 TPF` at the router, before any DB call (6M §28).
   - Internal tokens and anonymous callers → `DENY 401`.
   - PA → `ALLOW`, except AMI-6M-008/009 → `CONDITIONAL C-BG-SM`.
   - The lifecycle routes (AMI-6M-003 suspend, AMI-6M-004 reactivate) need no grant and no tenant membership (6M L137).
3. **DB binding.** `is_platform_admin()` requires `session_user = 'app_platform_admin'` AND the GUC (107_5B5). `EXECUTE` on `fn_break_glass_grant`/`release`/`check` is limited to `app_platform_admin`. `is_platform_admin()` is re-checked inside grant and release (FAR #14).
4. **Break-glass is additional, never a substitute** (FAR #14). A grant without a PA session is meaningless: `fn_break_glass_check` returns FALSE for non-PA.
5. **Read-oriented only.** 6B §18.4 L1006 puts only read-oriented break-glass access (plus forced-session revocation, a PA route) in scope, and 6B §31 L2343 denies PA on a tenant write. Every tenant write is therefore `DENY 403` in the PLATFORM_ADMIN column, whatever grant is presented. This replaces the earlier uniform `C-BG` cell with a route-by-route classification (§9.2).
6. **Binding.** A grant is bound to one organization, one admin, one session and an immutable `purposes` array. Reusing it across orgs, admins or sessions fails a six-check and returns the collapsed `BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` (AAM-ADV-08/09).
7. **Purpose scoping (AAM-OD-03 C).**
   - Allow-list: `SUPPORT_GENERAL`, `SUPPORT_BILLING`, `SUPPORT_ORG_LIFECYCLE`, `SUPPORT_QUOTA`, `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS`, `SUPPORT_SECURITY_INCIDENT`.
   - Wildcards are rejected at issuance. The default is `ARRAY['SUPPORT_GENERAL']`, never `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS` (105_5B4).
   - Each read-oriented tenant route requires the purpose mapped to it (6B §18.3b item 2, as amended). A grant with several purposes satisfies each route whose mapped purpose it contains.
   - `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS` and `SUPPORT_SECURITY_INCIDENT` satisfy no generic read.
   - Wrong purpose → `BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` (AAM-ADV-10, ADV-29).
8. **Status distinction preserved** (§7.3a). On a read-oriented tenant route: no header, a presented grant that fails, or wrong purpose → `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` (FAR L261 #10; AAM-F-07). On a tenant write, or on AMI-6D-011/014, whatever the header state → `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`. Redis down → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`.
9. **Audit.** Grant, release and sensitive-media access are audited in-transaction (107_5B5; `BREAK_GLASS_GRANTED`/`RELEASED`).
10. **`analytics_platform:read`** is held by no tenant role; AMI-6M-031 is PA-only (AAM-ADV-17).

WebSocket (§14): PLATFORM_ADMIN → `DENY` on AMI-WS-6D-002, AMI-WS-6D-003 and AMI-WS-6I-001. No baseline source defines a break-glass WebSocket mechanism, so the handshake fails closed.

## 10. Internal-Service Routes (4)

| AMI ID | Path | `token_use` | Service identity | Allow-list | `on_behalf_of` | Authorization | User JWT / API key / human PA token |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AMI-6C-041 | `/api/internal/v1/organizations/{organization_id}` | `internal` | `service_id` claim, internal issuer signature | per 6B internal allow-list | **required**; must equal the path org | `C-INT` (no permission string; a SERVICE principal has none) | `DENY 401` |
| AMI-6C-042 | `/api/internal/v1/organizations/{organization_id}/compliance-policy` | `internal` | as above | as above | **required**; must equal the path org | `C-INT` | `DENY 401` |
| AMI-6D-020 | `/api/internal/v1/calls/{call_id}` | `internal` | as above | as above | not used (cross-tenant support lookup) | `C-INT-PA`: issuer-asserted `actor_type=PLATFORM_ADMIN` | `DENY 401` |
| AMI-6E-017 | `/api/internal/v1/agents/{agent_id}/versions/{version_id}` | `internal` | as above | as above | not used | `C-INT-PA` | `DENY 401` |

Rules:
- Internal routes never accept a tenant JWT or an API key (FAR #10; 6D §34.2 L1834 requires tests for this).
- An internal token on any public route → `401` (6C §17). For AMI-6B-033 this is AAM-F-02, resolved by precedence.
- Internal tokens are not denylist-checked (6B) and are excluded from tenant quota.

## 11. Callback / Provider Trust Boundary (5)

| AMI ID | Path | Trust mechanism (actual) | Tenant resolution | Tenant JWT / key / PA / internal |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AMI-6B-013 | `GET /api/v1/auth/oauth/{provider}/callback` | OAuth `state` + authorization code (`C-STATE`) | from the redeemed state | `DENY SIG` |
| AMI-6D-021 | `POST /webhooks/voice/{provider_slug}/events` | Provider-native signature per adapter (6D §10.4 L404; 6A §28) (`C-PROV`) | after verification; event recorded in `webhooks.inbound_webhook_events` | `DENY SIG` |
| AMI-6J-011 | `GET /api/v1/integrations/oauth/{definition_key}/callback` | `fn_redeem_oauth_callback_state` single-use state (`C-STATE`) | from the redeemed state | `DENY SIG` |
| AMI-6J-014 | `POST /api/v1/integrations/providers/{provider_slug}/callbacks/{opaque_connection_route_id}` | Provider signature; opaque route id; tenant confirmed after verification (6J L1838) (`C-PROV`) | after verification | `DENY SIG` |
| AMI-6K-023 | `POST /api/v1/billing/payment-providers/{provider_slug}/webhook` | `PaymentProviderPort.verify_webhook` (6K §30) (`C-PROV`) | after verification | `DENY SIG` |

The voice callback path is exactly `/webhooks/voice/{provider_slug}/events`, with no `/api/v1` prefix. 6B/6D/6J/6K do not specify a uniform failure status for signature or state failures, so none is asserted here (AAM-I-15). A callback never accepts a bearer credential (tenant JWT, API key, PA token or internal token) as authentication (AAM-ADV-14).

## 12. Sensitive Media

| Asset | Route | Required (AND) | Roles ALLOW (computed) | API key | PLATFORM_ADMIN |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Recording audio (signed URL) | AMI-6D-011 | `recording:access_media` AND `recording:read` | ADMIN, OWNER | `C-KEY-SM` | `DENY 403` on this route; only via AMI-6M-008 with `C-BG-SM` |
| Transcript content | AMI-6D-014 | `transcript:access_content` AND `transcript:read` | ADMIN, OWNER | `C-KEY-SM` | `DENY 403` on this route; only via AMI-6M-009 with `C-BG-SM` |

1. `recording:read` and `transcript:read` are **metadata-only** (6D L731–732). VIEWER and MEMBER hold them and are denied media and content (AAM-ADV-01/02). The metadata routes AMI-6D-010/013 are read-oriented and so `C-BG-R-GENERAL` for PA. They never return bytes or content (AAM-ADV-26, ADV-32).
2. `call:read` never yields a signed URL (AAM-ADV-03).
3. A key needs the sensitive scope **explicitly**, and the issuer must have held it at issuance.
4. A custom role may hold the sensitive permission explicitly (6L L1205–1222: "MEMBER + custom role with sensitive permission → ALLOW"). That is `C-CR`, not inheritance. Under AAM-OD-02 A only OWNER or ADMIN, who hold it, can place it in a custom role. The subset rule governs custom roles only; system roles carry the sensitive permission exactly as the executed grants say (OWNER/ADMIN).
5. PA sensitive-media access requires all of:
   - purpose `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS`;
   - a grant that is valid, ACTIVE, unexpired and unreleased;
   - a grant bound to the org, admin, session and purpose;
   - `session_user='app_platform_admin'`;
   - an in-transaction audit record (6M §27.2; 107_5B5).

## 14. WebSocket Authorization Matrix (4 rows — not counted in the 369)

WebSocket handshakes do not use the REST status table blindly. In this table `DENY` means the handshake refuses the credential. | AAM-F-12 | P2 | RESOLVED BY RECONCILIATION (Option A) | The connection-sync authorization record had a one-shot TTL set at submission (Celery result lifetime + buffer): it did not cover queue time, run time or post-terminal retention, and a Celery `PENDING` (returned for any unknown or expired id) could be read as a live job; 6A §18.3 L486 still listed campaign `PREPARING` as a `/jobs` projection. Sub-invariant (pass 6): a stale `RUNNING` record could be classified worker-lost `FAILED` although the result backend already held a terminal result (e.g. `SUCCESS` stored, worker died before its Redis write) Sub-invariant (pass 7): the sender’s post-publication `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` write was described as part of submission, so a Redis failure, a slow sender or a worker that started first could be read as an enqueue failure and lead to a revoke, a record deletion or a `503` after the broker had already accepted the task — and a worker that had already advanced the record to `RUNNING` or `TERMINAL` could be regressed by that late write. | Reconciliation/implementation correction under AAM-OD-04 C and its supplement A (no new owner decision); controlled amendments 6J §15.3 L581, 6A §18.3 L488 (registry (iv), rule (9)) and L486 (§15.5); stale-reaper pattern of 3B/3C; Celery `task_postrun` ordering; no migration; pass 7 adds the monotonic compare-and-set state machine and the sender/worker confirmation race to the same amended line (6J §15.3 L581), with no new owner decision, no new finding and no migration | Lifecycle-bound Redis record (§7.3c): `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` → `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL`; enqueue failure → record deleted + `503`; queued durability by expiry `submitted_at + Q + L + S`; running lease renewed every `H`; worker terminal write by a post-persistence terminalizer with `terminal_at` = stored `date_done` and `result_available_until = terminal_at + R`; reconciler cleans orphans and, for a stale `RUNNING` record, **checks the result backend first**: a stored terminal result wins over the worker-lost fallback and is recovered with its stored status and `date_done` (`RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`), worker-lost `FAILED` only when the lookup succeeds with no terminal result, and an unavailable backend never yields a guessed `FAILED` (record stays `RUNNING`, bounded retry within `X`, then unclassified expiry + alert); `TERMINAL` is final against late writes; Celery consulted last on reads; Celery `PENDING` never proves existence; expired → 404; lost result / Redis down → 503. Four job types unchanged; `PREPARING` is not a job. Pass 7 (sender/worker confirmation race), twelve elements, all on 6J L581: (1) the record is written with `SET NX` **before** the broker publication and before the `202`, never after; (2) the post-publication `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` write is a single compare-and-set from `SUBMITTED` and is a **confirmation only**, carrying no authority of its own; (3) a failed or lost confirmation never revokes the task, never deletes the record and never converts an accepted submission into a `503`; (4) if the worker wins the race the record is already `RUNNING`, the compare-and-set fails and is abandoned; (5) if the job already reached `TERMINAL` the compare-and-set fails and the terminal state is never regressed; (6) transient contention is retried within the grace `G`, then abandoned; (7) a confirmation that cannot be written (Redis unavailable) is not evidence of a publication failure: nothing is revoked or deleted and the record stands under its queued expiry `submitted_at + Q + L + S`; (8) an absent record at confirmation time is an integrity failure — it is alerted and never recreated, because recreation would mint authority; (9) a corrupt or unparsable record fails closed (`503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`), never open; (10) a **definitive** broker-publication failure remains distinct and compensating: the record is deleted and the caller receives `503`, which is never confused with a lost or unobservable confirmation; (11) the worker establishes `RUNNING` by compare-and-set from `SUBMITTED` or `QUEUED` before any external side effect, so no side effect precedes a lifecycle write; (12) reads never write, and a Celery `PENDING` never proves existence. The transitions are exactly the five permitted and five rejected pairs of §7.3c; no route, permission, role, grant, job type or migration changed. | yes — cells, live sources and the §7.3c state machine confirm |

Open P0/P1/P2 findings: **0**. Open owner decisions: **0** (AAM-OD-01/02/03/04 and the AAM-OD-04 supplement decided, §15.3; AAM-F-12 needed no new decision).

### 15.3 Owner decisions (all four decided by the owner on 2026-09-19; not chosen by this document)

#### AAM-OD-01 — API key on `/users/me` (AMI-6C-038, 6C-039, 6C-040) — **DECIDED: A**

| Option | Behaviour | Impact |
|---|---|---|
| **A (selected)** | Keys are ineligible → `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED` | Consistent with the D1 self routes (6B-004/010/019–022). No user PII via keys; clients must use a user token. |
| B | Keys accepted; the self lookup resolves to no user (empty/404) | Not selected |
| C | Keys accepted; "me" resolves to the key's `created_by` user | Not selected (principal-boundary break) |

Applied in the generator: the three API_KEY cells are `DENY 403`. **No source edit was needed**, because the AMI and 6C rows state no key eligibility and the decision fills that gap as the narrowest reading. Closes AAM-F-06.

#### AAM-OD-02 — Custom-role creator/assigner subset rule (AAM-F-05) — **DECIDED: A**

| Option | Behaviour | Impact |
|---|---|---|
| **A (selected)** | A custom role (`is_system = false`) may be created, edited, assigned or invited only with permissions ⊆ the actor's own effective permissions. OWNER is unrestricted within the tenant catalogue. System-role assignment keeps the frozen hierarchy. | Closes the widening path; controlled amendment to 6B/6C (§15.5) |
| B | Accept as designed, with residual risk | Not selected |
| C | Restrict `role:manage` to OWNER | Not selected (a grant change would need a migration; it would also contradict 6B §31 L2333 "ADMIN POST roles → 201") |

Applied: `C-SUBSET` on AMI-6B-029 and 6B-031 (custom-role create/edit), and `C-ATTACH` on AMI-6C-010 and 6C-016 (role assignment and invitation: custom target → subset rule; system target → frozen hierarchy, so ADMIN may still assign or invite `BILLING_ADMIN`, `MEMBER`, `VIEWER` or `ADMIN`, and never `OWNER`). The rule is `target_role.is_system == false → permissions(target_role) ⊆ effective_permissions(actor)`. A custom role never confers PLATFORM_ADMIN, and `analytics_platform:read` is never grantable. Closes AAM-F-05; the over-broad first application (subset rule applied to system roles) is AAM-F-08.

#### AAM-OD-03 — Break-glass purpose for generic tenant reads (supersedes AAM-I-09) — **DECIDED: C**

| Option | Behaviour | Impact |
|---|---|---|
| A | Any valid grant (any purpose) satisfies every read-oriented tenant route | Not selected (purpose scoping would be meaningless outside media) |
| B | Only `SUPPORT_GENERAL` satisfies every read-oriented tenant route | Not selected |
| **C (selected)** | Domain-mapped purposes: quota read → `SUPPORT_QUOTA`; other 6K billing reads → `SUPPORT_BILLING`; the organization record → `SUPPORT_ORG_LIFECYCLE`; every other tenant read → `SUPPORT_GENERAL` | Controlled amendment to 6B §18.3b (§15.5); `C-BG-R-*` codes (§7.3, §9.2) |

#### AAM-OD-04 — Authorization of the generic job-status route AMI-6A-001 (supersedes AAM-I-12; AAM-F-09) — **DECIDED: C**

| Option | Behaviour | Impact |
|---|---|---|
| A | The read permission of the job's originating resource | Not selected |
| B | The permission of the operation that initiated the job | Not selected |
| **C (selected)** | Server-recorded initiator **or** the job type's explicit, server-defined `view_permission` (generic-job registry) | Controlled amendment to 6A §18.3 L488 (§15.5); `C-JOB` (§7.3); registry (§7.3b) |

The owner's binding text: "Authorization metadata must be server-generated and server-stored when the job is created. Never trust a client-provided initiator ID, resource type, required permission, or authorization policy." Its seven rules, as encoded in `C-JOB`:

1. Authentication and tenant checks run first.
2. A job belonging to another organization → `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`.
3. Allow if the caller is the server-recorded initiator, or holds the job type's server-defined `view_permission`.
4. The initiator gets no bypass of session, membership, tenant isolation, principal-type eligibility or global controls.
5. PLATFORM_ADMIN, API-key, internal and other principals gain nothing from an ambiguous initiator comparison: the match is on principal type **and** id.
6. No client-controlled or inferred metadata.
7. No fallback to a broad generic permission.
8. PLATFORM_ADMIN never satisfies rule 3; it reads a job only through read-only `SUPPORT_GENERAL` break-glass after the job's organization is resolved (foreign → 404), with no initiator shortcut and no tenant `view_permission`, audited (6A L488 rule 8).

Schema check (no migration): of the job tables 6A §18.3 names, only `campaign.csv_import_jobs` has a creator column (`created_by`, 028_5E), and it is a bare UUID with no recorded principal type. Rule 5 requires an exact match on principal type and id, so `created_by` is **not** an initiator; `knowledge.ingestion_jobs` (037_5F) and `campaign.call_jobs` (031_5E) record none. No V1 registry entry supports an initiator, and every job type is decided by its `view_permission` (§7.3b). Applied in the generator: AMI-6A-001 role cells → `CONDITIONAL C-JOB`; custom role → `C-CR+C-JOB`; key → `C-KEY+C-JOB`; PA → `C-BG-R-GENERAL` (read-only break-glass, never `C-JOB`); internal → `DENY 401`.

#### AAM-OD-04 supplement — `integrations.connection_sync` (6J §15.3, AMI-6J-013) — **DECIDED: A**

The connection-sync job had no server-stored tenant or type (a Celery task id only). Owner decision (2026-09-19): option **A**, a server-controlled Redis authorization record, no migration. Encoded in 6J L581 (controlled amendment, §15.5): the server generates the `job_id`; before the `202` it writes `jobauth:{organization_id}:{job_id}` holding `organization_id`, `job_type = integrations.connection_sync` and `connection_id`, all server-generated; the record is lifecycle-bound (§7.3c; refined in pass 5 under AAM-F-12, replacing the original submission-relative TTL); `GET /api/v1/jobs/{job_id}` takes organization, type and state from the record and reads the result backend only after authorization; a Celery `PENDING` never proves existence; missing or expired record → `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`; Redis unavailable → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` (fail closed, not not-found); no initiator shortcut; `view_permission` = `integration:read`, the permission of `GET /api/v1/integrations/connections/{connection_id}` (AMI-6J-005); API keys only via `C-KEY`; PLATFORM_ADMIN only via break-glass; cross-tenant → 404. No migration 113 and no `IntegrationSyncJob` table. Pass 7 makes the lifecycle a monotonic compare-and-set state machine on that same line: every write names its predecessor state, the sender’s post-publication `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` write is a confirmation that never revokes the task, deletes the record or regresses a worker that already reached `RUNNING` or `TERMINAL`, a definitive publication failure stays a distinct compensating delete + `503`, an absent record is alerted and never recreated, and a corrupt record fails closed. Still no migration 113 and no `IntegrationSyncJob` table.

### 15.4 Adversarial cases (each verified against §13/§14 by the assembler; the "Verified" column is computed)
