| AAM-ADV-97 | Stale `RUNNING`, backend lookup succeeds with no terminal result | **Worker-lost fallback allowed: `TERMINAL`/`FAILED`, `RECONCILER`, reconciliation time** | LC-22; race checks | yes |
| AAM-ADV-98 | Stale `RUNNING` while the result backend is unavailable | **No terminal write and no guessed `FAILED`; stays `RUNNING`; bounded retry (`X`); then unclassified expiry + alert** | LC-23, LC-26; race checks | yes |
| AAM-ADV-99 | Late worker / terminalizer write after the reconciler recovered or failed the job | **Ignored: compare-and-set from a non-terminal state only; `TERMINAL` never regressed or replaced** | LC-08, LC-25; race checks | yes |
| AAM-ADV-100 | Fallback code attempts worker-lost `FAILED` while the backend holds `SUCCESS` | **Prevented: the backend is queried first and a stored result always takes precedence** | LC-20, LC-21, LC-25; race checks | yes |
| AAM-ADV-101 | Task body writes `TERMINAL` before Celery persisted the result, then the process dies | **Not possible: the terminal write is made by a post-persistence terminalizer, never the task body** | LC-24; race checks | yes |

| AAM-ADV-103 | Worker dequeues and starts before the sender writes its `QUEUED` confirmation | **Worker wins: `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` by compare-and-set; the sender’s `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` compare-and-set fails and is abandoned; the `202` stands** | LC-27, LC-29, LC-33, LC-40 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-104 | Job completes before the sender writes its confirmation | **`TERMINAL` is never regressed: the late `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` compare-and-set fails and is abandoned; the result stays readable until `result_available_until`** | LC-27, LC-28, LC-34 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-105 | Sender retries the confirmation under transient contention | **Bounded retry within the grace `G`, then abandoned; no revoke, no delete, no state regression** | LC-35, LC-41 | yes |

| AAM-ADV-107 | The authorization record is corrupt or unparsable at confirmation time | **Fails closed: `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; never treated as an absent record and never overwritten open** | LC-38 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-108 | A late sender confirmation attempts `RUNNING` → `QUEUED` | **Rejected by the compare-and-set: the lifecycle is monotonic and no writer regresses a started job** | LC-27, LC-28 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-109 | A late writer attempts `TERMINAL` → `QUEUED` or `TERMINAL` → `RUNNING` | **Rejected: `TERMINAL` is final against the sender, the worker and the reconciler** | LC-28, LC-34 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-110 | Any writer attempts to rewind a record to `SUBMITTED` | **Rejected: `SUBMITTED` is written once, by `SET NX`, before publication; no writer recreates or rewinds it** | LC-28, LC-29, LC-37 | yes |

| AAM-ADV-112 | The confirmation is treated as a transactional prerequisite of the `202` | **Rejected by design: the submission is complete once the record exists and the publication is confirmed; the confirmation is not a prerequisite** | LC-31, LC-39 | yes |

| AAM-ADV-114 | Reconciler observes a `SUBMITTED` record older than the grace `G` with no worker activity | **Bounded cleanup of the unstarted record after `G`; the residual window of an unwritten confirmation is bounded by `G` and never unbounded** | LC-36, LC-41 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-115 | Two workers attempt `RUNNING` on the same record | **Exactly one compare-and-set succeeds; the loser does not run and performs no side effect; the lease `now + L + S` is held by the winner** | LC-29, LC-35, LC-40 | yes |

| AAM-ADV-119 | PLATFORM_ADMIN presents an expired or released grant | **Canonical break-glass failure; the invalid grant is never used as a resource-location namespace and no lookup is performed on its behalf** | 6A L488 rule (8a)(c); §7.3d 4 | yes |

| AAM-ADV-121 | The grant’s `admin_user_id` or `session_id` does not match the calling admin or session | **Canonical break-glass failure at the pre-resource checks; no lookup, no organization derived, no existence signal** | 6B §18.3a; 6A L488 rule (8a)(b)–(c); §7.3d 4 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-122 | PLATFORM_ADMIN submits `organization_id` = A in the query string or body while the grant targets organization B | **The client value is ignored entirely; the namespace is the stored `grant.organization_id` (B), so the lookup is `jobauth:{B}:{job_id}` and a job in A is `404`** | 6A L488 rule (8a)(d); §7.3d 5; §8 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-123 | Redis is unavailable while a PLATFORM_ADMIN resolves a connection-sync job under a valid grant | **`503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `404`; there is no fallback to the Celery result backend and no guessed state** | 6J §15.3 L581 (6); 6A L488 rules (9)/(8a)(j); §7.3d | yes |
| AAM-ADV-124 | PLATFORM_ADMIN supplies an arbitrary Celery task id that exists in the result backend but has no `jobauth` record in the grant’s organization | **`404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`: the result backend is consulted only after the tenant-scoped record is resolved and the break-glass authorization completes; `PENDING` never proves existence** | 6J §15.3 L581 (9); 6A L488 rule (9); §7.3d 6–10 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-125 | The record resolved under the candidate namespace carries an `organization_id` different from the grant’s (corruption or a misplaced write) | **Fails closed: no tenant context, no job state, no existence signal; the existing integrity/security alert path is raised and no new public error code is minted** | 6A L488 rule (8a)(g)–(h); §7.3d 9 | yes |
| AAM-ADV-126 | An implementation tries to locate the job globally — `SCAN jobauth:*:{job_id}`, per-organization iteration or a reverse `job_id` index | **Forbidden by the contract: exactly one candidate namespace is derived from one grant, and no global or cross-tenant search exists to be abused** | 6A L488 rule (8a)(e); §7.3d “Forbidden”; Gate ZA | yes |

### 15.5 Controlled-amendment ledger

The owner decisions authorised every amendment. Each is a **single-line** edit: text is appended to an existing line or, for this document's own earlier amendment text, rewritten within that same line, so no line number cited anywhere in the corpus shifts. No route, count, permission, role, grant, status code or migration changed.

access is granted when the entry supports an initiator and the caller is the job's **server-recorded initiator** (an exact match on the principal type and principal id recorded by the server when the job was created), **or** when the caller holds the entry's **server-defined `view_permission`** (an API key only through its effective, scope-limited permissions); otherwise `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`; (4) being the initiator bypasses no session, membership, tenant-isolation, principal-type or global control; (5) a platform-admin, API-key, internal or other principal gains nothing from an ambiguous initiator comparison — the comparison matches only an identical principal type and id; (6) the initiator id, resource type, required permission and authorization policy are server-generated and server-stored when the job is created, and are never taken from, or inferred from, client input; (7) there is no fallback to a broad generic permission, and a job whose server-resolved type has no registry entry is denied; (8) `PLATFORM_ADMIN` is not a tenant principal and never satisfies rule (3): it may read a job only through the purpose-scoped, read-only break-glass path (`SUPPORT_GENERAL`, 6B §18.3a), after the server has resolved the job's organization and that organization is the grant's target organization — a job in any other organization is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`; the initiator shortcut and the tenant `view_permission` never apply to it, and the read is audited; (9) a job exists only while its registry source (row or server-written record) exists: a result-backend state such as Celery `PENDING` never proves, creates or resurrects a job, an absent or retention-expired source is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, and an unavailable source fails closed with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `404` and never a guessed status. **Generic-job registry (normative for V1; AAM-OD-04 supplement, owner decision 2026-09-19).** Every entry: type, tenant and permission are resolved server-side and are never supplied or overridden by the client. (i) `knowledge.ingestion_job` — owner 6F (ingestion pipeline started by AMI-6F-008, AMI-6F-009 URL/website and AMI-6F-012 reprocess); source `knowledge.ingestion_jobs` (037_5F); tenant = the row's `organization_id` under RLS; initiator not supported (no initiator column); `view_permission` = `knowledge:read`. (ii) `campaign.csv_import_job` — owner 6H (created by AMI-6H-017, processed via AMI-6H-018); source `campaign.csv_import_jobs` (028_5E); tenant = the row's `organization_id` under RLS; initiator not supported: `created_by` is a bare UUID with no recorded principal type, so it is not an initiator and is never compared with a user or API-key id; `view_permission` = `campaign:read`. (iii) `campaign.call_job` — owner 6H (created only by the campaign executor); source `campaign.call_jobs` (031_5E); tenant = the row's `organization_id` under RLS; initiator not supported; `view_permission` = `campaign:read`. (iv) `integrations.connection_sync` — owner 6J §15.3 (AMI-6J-013); source the server-written Redis authorization record defined in 6J §15.3 (`organization_id`, `job_type`, `connection_id`), whose server-controlled lifecycle (`SUBMITTED`/`QUEUED` → `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL`, kept until `result_available_until` = actual terminal time + the result-backend retention, then expired) alone establishes the job's existence and status, the Celery result backend supplying only terminal detail after authorization; tenant = the record's `organization_id`; initiator not supported; `view_permission` = `integration:read`. Campaign `PREPARING` materialization, named above, mints no `job_id` and is not a job type; a future export feature has no job type until its owning contract adds a registry entry by controlled amendment. **⚠ CONTROLLED CLARIFICATION (API Authorization Matrix phase, 2026-09-20; under owner decision AAM-OD-04 C and its `integrations.connection_sync` supplement (option A) — no new owner decision, no new route, no new permission, no new public error code, no new table, no new index and no migration) — rule (8a), the `PLATFORM_ADMIN` resolution order for a registry source keyed by organization:** rule (3) resolves the job inside the caller's already-established tenant context and rule (8) requires the job's organization to be known before the break-glass target-organization match, but a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` has no tenant context, so where the registered backing source is keyed by organization — such as the `integrations.connection_sync` authorization record `jobauth:{organization_id}:{job_id}` of 6J §15.3 — rule (8) as written would need the organization in order to find the record that carries the organization. The resolution is therefore two-stage, and the missing organization is taken from the server's own stored break-glass authorization metadata, never from the caller. (a) **Tenant principals are unchanged**: a system-role user, a custom-role user or an API key is resolved inside its authenticated tenant context, the job is looked up only in that organization, and rules (2)–(7) apply exactly as written. (b) For a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` the server authenticates the principal normally, requires the `X-Break-Glass-Grant` reference of 6B §18.3a, loads that grant **server-side** from `organization.break_glass_grants` through the §18.3a validation path, and evaluates every grant check that does not depend on the resource: the grant exists, its `status` is `ACTIVE` and not `RELEASED`, it is not past `expires_at`, its `admin_user_id` is the calling admin, its `session_id` is the calling session, and its purpose is the `SUPPORT_GENERAL` this route requires. (c) A grant reference that is absent, malformed or unresolvable is `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` and never `404`; an invalid, expired, released, revoked, wrong-purpose or actor/session-mismatched grant is the canonical 6B §18.3a break-glass failure; a grant failing any check in (b) is **never** used to locate a resource, and no lookup of any kind is performed on its behalf. (d) The checks in (b) do **not** complete break-glass authorization; they make the stored grant trustworthy enough for the server to read `candidate_organization_id = grant.organization_id` **from the stored grant row**, which is a resource-location namespace and not a permission. That candidate organization is never taken from, defaulted from or overridden by a query parameter, a request body, any header other than the server-resolved grant reference, a route variable, client-submitted job metadata, or caller-supplied Celery task metadata; a client-supplied organization identifier is ignored entirely. (e) The job is then resolved **only** within that one organization — for `integrations.connection_sync` exactly the key `jobauth:{grant.organization_id}:{job_id}`, and for a table-backed entry exactly `WHERE organization_id = grant.organization_id AND job_id = :job_id`. A global or cross-tenant search is forbidden: no `SCAN jobauth:*:{job_id}`, no iteration over organizations, no querying each tenant's table until something matches, no global reverse index from `job_id` to organization, and no automatic retry against a second break-glass grant. One request and one grant yield exactly one candidate namespace. (f) If no record exists under that candidate namespace the answer is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, identical to a job that never existed; a job that exists only in another organization is therefore indistinguishable from a nonexistent one, and concealment holds because the other organization is never searched. (g) If a record is resolved, its organization is read from the **server-stored** record itself and the mandatory break-glass resource binding of rule (8) still runs: `resource.organization_id == grant.organization_id`. The candidate namespace narrows the search; it never substitutes for this check. (h) If a backing source nevertheless returns a record whose embedded organization differs from the candidate grant organization, the request fails closed — no tenant context, no job state, no existence signal — and the existing integrity/security alert path of that source contract is raised; no new public error code is introduced for this corrupt-data case. (i) Only after the complete break-glass authorization has succeeded may the server establish a tenant context for the request, expose the job's lifecycle state, consult the Celery result backend under rule (9), or emit the 6B §18.3a break-glass audit event, which records the grant id and the resolved target organization. (j) A dependency failure keeps the semantics of rule (9) and of 6J §15.3: an unavailable backing source is `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `404` and never a guessed state, and a dependency failure is never converted into an existence answer. (k) None of this gives a `PLATFORM_ADMIN` a tenant role, tenant membership, the tenant path of rule (3), a tenant `view_permission` such as `integration:read` held as a tenant principal, or the initiator shortcut; the read remains the purpose-scoped, read-only, audited break-glass read of rule (8). |

pass 6: stale-`RUNNING` reconciliation checks the result backend first (stored result wins over the worker-lost fallback, backend unavailable never guesses `FAILED`) and the terminal write moves to a post-persistence terminalizer; pass 7: the lifecycle is made a monotonic compare-and-set state machine and the sender/worker post-publication confirmation race is closed (the `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` write is a confirmation only — never a revoke, a delete, a `503` or a regression of a worker that already reached `RUNNING` or `TERMINAL`; a definitive publication failure stays a distinct compensating delete + `503`; an absent record is alerted and never recreated; a corrupt record fails closed) | **⚠ CONTROLLED AMENDMENT (API Authorization Matrix phase, 2026-09-19; owner decision AAM-OD-04 supplement, option A) — job authorization record:** (1) the server generates the `job_id` (the Celery task ID); the client never chooses or alters it; (2) before returning `202`, and before the task is enqueued, the server writes a Redis authorization record `jobauth:{organization_id}:{job_id}` containing `organization_id` (from the auth context), `job_type = integrations.connection_sync` and `connection_id` (from the resolved connection) — all server-generated, none client-supplied or overridable; if the write fails, no task is enqueued and no `202` is returned; (3) the record is **lifecycle-bound, never submission-relative** (finding AAM-F-12): besides the fields in (2) it carries server-written lifecycle fields only — internal `state` (`SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` → `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL`; a record past `result_available_until`, or absent, is `EXPIRED`), `submitted_at`, `started_at`, `lease_expires_at`, `terminal_status` (`SUCCEEDED` or `FAILED`; cancellation is a V1 non-goal), `terminal_source` (`WORKER`, `RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY` or `RECONCILER`), `terminal_at` and `result_available_until` — and it is created with `SET NX` and never overwritten by a new submission; every duration is server configuration, never client input: `R` = the Celery result-backend retention (`result_expires`), `Q` = the maximum queue age, `T` = the task hard time limit, `L` = the running lease, `H` = the worker heartbeat interval (`H ≤ L/3`), `I` = the reconciler interval (`I ≤ L/3`), `S` = a safety margin (`S ≥ I` plus clock tolerance), `G` = the enqueue-confirmation grace and `X` = the result-recovery grace (`X ≥ 2I`, and `R > L + S + X`, so a stored result is still retained when the reconciler looks for it); (3a) **submission**: the record is written in `SUBMITTED` with key expiry `submitted_at + Q + L + S`, then the task is published to the broker with Celery `expires = submitted_at + Q`, so no worker can start it later; the lifecycle is **monotonic** — the only permitted transitions are `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` (the sender's post-publication confirmation), `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` and `QUEUED` → `RUNNING` (a worker starting work), `QUEUED` → `TERMINAL` (never started) and `RUNNING` → `TERMINAL` (terminalizer or reconciler); every transition is a compare-and-set from a named predecessor state and no actor ever writes a state unconditionally, so `RUNNING` → `QUEUED`, `TERMINAL` → `QUEUED`, `TERMINAL` → `RUNNING`, `RUNNING` → `SUBMITTED` and `QUEUED` → `SUBMITTED` are rejected and no state ever moves backwards; if the publication **definitively fails** — the broker rejects it or does not accept it — the server deletes the `SUBMITTED` record synchronously and returns `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`, never `202`, and no task executes; after a publication that **succeeded**, the sender attempts one compare-and-set `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED`, which is a monotonic *confirmation* and not authority over a job that has already progressed: the sender never revokes, deletes or overwrites a record because its own compare-and-set lost, and it interprets the outcome by reading the authoritative record — (i) the compare-and-set succeeds, the state is `QUEUED` and the server returns `202`; (ii) the record is already `QUEUED` (a retried or duplicate confirmation), which is idempotently successful, is not rewritten, and returns `202`; (iii) the record is already `RUNNING`, i.e. a fast worker legitimately moved `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` before the confirmation — the worker winning that race is a **successful submission**, `202`, and `RUNNING` is preserved: no revoke, no delete, no regression to `QUEUED` and no false `503`; (iv) the record is already `TERMINAL`, i.e. the task also finished before the confirmation — this is likewise a successful submission, `202`, and the terminal result is preserved unchanged and never regressed to `QUEUED`; (v) the record is still `SUBMITTED` and the compare-and-set lost to transient contention, in which case the sender retries the read-and-compare-and-set sequence a bounded number of times within the request and never deletes or revokes the job merely because one attempt failed; (vi) Redis is unavailable for the confirmation although the prewrite and the publication both succeeded — a confirmation that cannot be written is never evidence that publication failed, so the server deletes and revokes nothing, keeps the accepted submission, leaves the worker free to move `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING` and the reconciler free to handle a record left in `SUBMITTED` under (3e), and `GET /api/v1/jobs/{job_id}` answers `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` for as long as Redis itself is unavailable, per (6); (vii) the record is absent while Redis is reachable, which is an integrity failure after publication: the server never recreates it from Celery, raises an integrity alert, makes a best-effort revoke of the published task and returns the dependency-failure response rather than claiming success — the worker rule of (3c) already prevents work without a record; (viii) any other or corrupt state fails closed with an integrity alert, with no overwrite, no delete and no manufactured lifecycle data; the post-publication confirmation is therefore never a transactional prerequisite for accepting the job, a definitive publication failure (record deleted, `503`, nothing executes) stays distinct from a confirmation that merely lost the race or could not be observed (`202`, record preserved), and a client is never told to retry a sync that was in fact accepted; (3b) **queued**: the key expiry written at submission already outlives the latest possible start (`submitted_at + Q`) by `L + S`, so a queued job stays durable before any worker starts, with no client heartbeat and no renewal; (3c) **running**: before any provider-visible or otherwise external side effect the worker must itself establish `RUNNING`, moving the record by compare-and-set from an allowed predecessor — from `SUBMITTED` when it starts before the sender's confirmation, or from `QUEUED` — and it never creates or recreates a record: receipt of the Celery message alone never authorizes work; if the record is absent, expired, belongs to another job, is in a state from which `RUNNING` is not reachable, or cannot be safely consulted because Redis is unavailable, the worker does no work, invents no authorization metadata and releases the `integrationsync:{connection_id}` lock, and it does the same when that compare-and-set fails because the record is no longer `SUBMITTED` or `QUEUED`; while running, the worker renews `lease_expires_at` and the key expiry to `now + L + S` every `H`, and stops renewing at `started_at + T`; each renewal is a compare-and-set on `RUNNING`, and a worker whose renewal finds the record no longer `RUNNING` abandons the task; (3d) **terminal**: only after the task result is stored in the result backend, the worker moves the record from `RUNNING` to `TERMINAL` by compare-and-set; this write is made by a worker-side terminalizer that runs after Celery has persisted the task's terminal state (a `task_postrun` signal handler), never by the task body, which cannot observe that persistence; the terminalizer reads the stored result back by the server-generated task id and takes `terminal_status` from it (`SUCCESS` → `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILURE` → `FAILED`), with `terminal_source = WORKER`, `terminal_at` = the backend's completion time (`date_done`), `result_available_until = terminal_at + R` and key expiry `result_available_until + S`, so the record outlives the whole result-retention window measured from the actual terminal time, not from submission; if the stored result cannot be read back, or no handler runs (for example after a hard-time-limit kill, where the pool stores the failure), the terminalizer writes nothing and the reconciler recovers the stored result under (3e); Redis therefore never claims a terminal state earlier than the stored result that fixes `terminal_at` and `result_available_until`; `TERMINAL` is final and any later write is ignored: every terminal write is a compare-and-set from a non-terminal state, so a late worker, terminalizer or reconciler write never regresses or replaces a `TERMINAL` record; (3e) **orphans**: a periodic server reconciler (APScheduler, the stale-reaper pattern of 3B and 3C) deletes a `SUBMITTED` record older than `G`, moves a `QUEUED` record past `submitted_at + Q` to `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` (not started) with `terminal_source = RECONCILER`, `terminal_at` = the reconciliation time and `result_available_until = terminal_at + R`; a `RUNNING` record whose lease has expired, or that is past `started_at + T + L`, is stale, and for it the reconciler **queries the result backend first**, by the record's server-generated task id, before any worker-lost classification: (i) **stored terminal result** — if the backend holds a terminal result for that task (`SUCCESS` or `FAILURE`), the reconciler moves the record from `RUNNING` to `TERMINAL` by compare-and-set with the stored outcome as `terminal_status` (`SUCCESS` → `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILURE` → `FAILED`), `terminal_source = RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`, `terminal_at` = the stored result's `date_done`, never the reconciliation time, and `result_available_until = terminal_at + R`, so a job the backend stored as succeeded is never reported as worker-lost `FAILED`; (ii) **no terminal result** — only when the lookup succeeds and the backend holds no terminal result for that task does the reconciler move the record to `TERMINAL`/`FAILED` (worker lost), with `terminal_source = RECONCILER`, `terminal_at` = the reconciliation time and `result_available_until = terminal_at + R`; (iii) **result backend unavailable** — if the lookup fails or the backend is unreachable, the reconciler writes no terminal state and guesses no outcome: the record stays `RUNNING`, a dependency alert is raised and the reconciler retries on its next run, extending the key expiry to at most `now + I + S` and never beyond `started_at + T + L + S + X`; if the backend is still unavailable at that bound the record expires unclassified (the job is then `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, its durable outcome remains on `GET .../connections/{connection_id}`) and an integrity alert is raised; if the reconciler is itself down, the finite key expiry still removes the record, so no record is immortal (lifetime at most `Q + T + L + X + R + 2S`) and no job stays `PENDING` forever; (4) `GET /api/v1/jobs/{job_id}` resolves organization, job type and lifecycle state from this record, and consults the Celery result backend only after authentication, record resolution, the organization match and the permission check, under the 6A §18.3 generic-job registry: `view_permission` = `integration:read` (the permission of `GET .../connections/{connection_id}`); no initiator shortcut; an API key only through its effective, scope-limited permissions; `PLATFORM_ADMIN` only through the read-only break-glass path; (5) a missing or expired record is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, identical to a nonexistent or cross-tenant job; (6) Redis unavailability is never treated as not-found: the request fails closed with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; (7) no migration and no `IntegrationSyncJob` table is added in V1; (8) the public status comes from the record — `SUBMITTED` and `QUEUED` → `PENDING`, `RUNNING` → `RUNNING`, `TERMINAL` → its `terminal_status` — and no new public status is introduced; the result backend may only supply the detail (`result_ref`, `error`) of a `TERMINAL` job whose outcome it stored (`terminal_source` `WORKER` or `RECONCILER_RESULT_RECOVERY`); a `RUNNING` record reaches `TERMINAL` only through the terminalizer of (3d) or the reconciler of (3e), and a stored backend terminal result always takes precedence over the worker-lost fallback; the read path never writes the record, never creates a job and never moves a state backwards; a `TERMINAL` record with `terminal_source = RECONCILER` (not started or worker lost) is answered as `FAILED` from the record alone, and a backend result stored after it never replaces it; (9) Celery reports `PENDING` for any unknown or expired task id, so a Celery `PENDING` never proves that a job exists: with no record the answer is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`, never `PENDING`, and no record is ever recreated from Celery; once `result_available_until` has passed (within the clock tolerance) the job is `404 RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND` — the durable outcome remains on `GET .../connections/{connection_id}`; a worker-written `TERMINAL` result that the backend no longer returns before `result_available_until`, or a result backend that is unreachable, is answered with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE` and raises an integrity alert, never a false `PENDING`. |

(b) The record is created with `SET NX` before the broker publication and before the `202`. (c) The post-publication `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` write is a confirmation only and carries no authority. (d) A failed, lost or unwritable confirmation never revokes the task, never deletes the record and never turns an accepted submission into a `503`. (e) A worker that wins the race takes `SUBMITTED` → `RUNNING`; the sender’s compare-and-set then fails and is abandoned. (f) An early `TERMINAL` is never regressed. (g) Transient contention is retried within the grace `G` and the residual window is bounded by `G`. (h) An absent record at confirmation time is alerted and never recreated. (i) A corrupt record fails closed with `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`. (j) A definitive broker-publication failure remains distinct and compensating (revoke if it may exist, delete the record, `503`). (k) The worker establishes `RUNNING` by compare-and-set before any external side effect. No route, permission, role, grant, job type, 6A line or migration changed; §7.3c gains the transition table and the lifecycle invariants are re-derived from the live sources. | Reconciliation under AAM-OD-04 C and its supplement A (no new owner decision) |

## 17. Test-Generation Readiness (no code)

A test generator can derive one case per (row × column) cell of §13/§14:

| Cell | Test to generate |
|---|---|
| `ALLOW` | Principal in its own tenant with the stated credential → expect the route's success status (per the owning contract). Also a foreign-tenant variant → expect 404 (tenant rows). |
| `DENY 403` | Expect `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`, plus a foreign-tenant variant → 404 (the tenant gate precedes the permission gate). In the PLATFORM_ADMIN column (tenant writes and AMI-6D-011/014), test without a header, with an invalid grant and with a valid grant of every purpose: all expect `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`. |
| `DENY 403 TPF` | Expect `403 TENANT_PRINCIPAL_FORBIDDEN` with no DB side effect. |
| `DENY 401*` | Expect the 401 family per 6B §22. |
| `DENY SIG` | Present the bearer credential without a valid signature/state → expect the owning contract's failure status; assert no tenant side effect. |
| `CONDITIONAL` | One positive case with every condition satisfied, and one negative case per condition code with the failure status in §7.3. |
| `CONDITIONAL C-BG-R-<P>` | Positive: a valid grant containing purpose P (alone, and inside a multi-purpose grant). Negatives: no header → `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID` (FAR L261 #10); each of the six checks failing → `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID`; a grant carrying every other allow-listed purpose but not P → `403 BREAK_GLASS_GRANT_INVALID`; Redis unavailable → `503 DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`. |
| `CONDITIONAL …C-SUBSET` | Custom roles only. Positive: a role whose permissions ⊆ the actor's set. Negative: include one permission the actor lacks (for ADMIN: each of `billing:manage`, `tax:manage`, `organization:delete`, `organization:suspend`) → `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`. For OWNER: `analytics_platform:read` → rejected. |
| `CONDITIONAL …C-ATTACH` | System target: ADMIN assigns or invites `ADMIN`, `MEMBER`, `BILLING_ADMIN`, `VIEWER` → success; `OWNER` target → rejected (409 `ROLE_CHANGE_NOT_ALLOWED` on 6C-010; `role_id` validation on 6C-016). Custom target: the `C-SUBSET` positive and negatives. |
| `CONDITIONAL …C-JOB` | For each registry type (§7.3b): a caller holding its `view_permission` → 200; a caller lacking it → 403 `AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`; a foreign-org job → 404; a nonexistent job → 404. A caller whose user id equals a CSV job's `created_by` but who lacks `campaign:read` → 403 (no initiator in V1). A job type absent from the registry → denied. Client-supplied type, permission or initiator fields are ignored. PLATFORM_ADMIN → break-glass (`SUPPORT_GENERAL`) only. Connection sync: missing/expired record → 404; Redis unavailable → 503 `DEPENDENCY_UNAVAILABLE`; queued job polled → `PENDING`; running job past any fixed TTL still readable; unknown id with Celery `PENDING` → 404; after `result_available_until` → 404; result lost before it → 503; enqueue failure → 503 and no record; worker never recreates a deleted record; stale `RUNNING` with a stored `SUCCESS` → `SUCCEEDED` with the stored `date_done`, never worker-lost `FAILED`; stale `RUNNING` with a stored `FAILURE` → `FAILED` with the stored `date_done`; stale `RUNNING`, empty successful lookup → worker-lost `FAILED`; stale `RUNNING`, backend unavailable → no transition; late write after `TERMINAL` → ignored (§7.3c). |
| `N/A` | No case required. Optionally assert that presenting the credential grants nothing beyond the anonymous result. |

Required negative suites:
- all 126 cases of §15.4;
- revoked or expired key → 401 at authentication;
- key ceiling (issuer lacks the permission at issuance → 403 even if scoped);
- each break-glass six-check failing individually;
- the WebSocket subscribe-time re-authorisation and force-revoke (§14).

No cell is blocked on an owner decision.

## 18. Security Invariants

1. Role cells derive only from executed grants; no prose table can grant.
2. No tenant principal (system role, custom role or API key) reaches `/api/v1/platform-admin/*`.
3. PLATFORM_ADMIN never satisfies tenant RBAC. Tenant data requires a valid break-glass grant, and break-glass never substitutes for PA.
4. Break-glass on tenant routes is read-only. Every tenant write denies PA with `403 AUTHORIZATION_DENIED`, whatever grant is presented.
5. Every read-oriented break-glass request needs its mapped purpose. `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS` and `SUPPORT_SECURITY_INCIDENT` never satisfy a generic read. A grant never implies sensitive media.
6. Sensitive media requires the explicit sensitive permission (or scope) and never follows from `recording:read`, `transcript:read` or `call:read`. PA sensitive media requires `SENSITIVE_MEDIA_ACCESS`, a bound, active, unexpired and unreleased grant, and an audit record, and is reachable only through AMI-6M-008/009.
7. API-key effective permissions = scopes ∩ issuer permissions at issuance. Keys are org-bound. A revoked or expired key → 401. Keys are never accepted on PA, internal, key-ineligible or `/users/me` routes.
8. No principal can confer, through a custom role, a permission it cannot itself exercise. OWNER is bounded by the tenant catalogue. System roles are assigned under the frozen hierarchy (ADMIN: any non-OWNER system role; OWNER only by transfer). A custom role never confers PLATFORM_ADMIN.
9. Internal routes accept only internal tokens, and internal tokens are rejected on public routes.
10. Callbacks authenticate only by provider signature or state. The tenant is resolved after verification.
11. Restricted tokens are valid only at their designated endpoint.
12. Foreign-tenant resources return 404, never 403.
13. Custom roles have no inheritance.
14. Guards only narrow access. Quotas are never authorization, and frontend behaviour is never a control.
15. Status codes are only those in 6B §22, FAR and 6M §28; none are invented.
16. Job status (AMI-6A-001) is authorized only from server-stored job metadata and the generic-job registry (§7.3b): a supported server-recorded initiator (none in V1) or the registry `view_permission`, after tenant checks; never from client input, never through a generic fallback, and never through `C-JOB` for PLATFORM_ADMIN, which uses read-only break-glass (AAM-OD-04 C and supplement A).
17. A job exists only while its registry source exists (6A L488 rule 9). The connection-sync Redis record is lifecycle-bound (§7.3c): durable while queued, lease-renewed while running, retained until `result_available_until` = actual terminal time + `R`, and cleaned up by the reconciler or finite expiry; for a stale `RUNNING` record the reconciler checks the result backend first, a stored terminal result (with its `date_done`) beats the worker-lost fallback, and an unavailable backend never yields a guessed `FAILED`. A Celery `PENDING` never proves, creates or resurrects a job; Redis or result-backend unavailability is `503`, never `404` or a guessed status. The lifecycle is monotonic: every write is a compare-and-set from a named predecessor state, the sender’s post-publication `SUBMITTED` → `QUEUED` write is a confirmation that carries no authority and never revokes, deletes or regresses a record, no backward transition is accepted (`RUNNING` → `QUEUED`, `TERMINAL` → `QUEUED`, `TERMINAL` → `RUNNING`, `RUNNING` → `SUBMITTED`, `QUEUED` → `SUBMITTED`), and a worker establishes `RUNNING` by compare-and-set before any external side effect.

| Gate | Check | Result | Evidence (computed) |

## 20. Reproducibility

### 20.1 Method

1. Parse the AMI §5/§6 tables into JSON (`parse_ami.py`). Rows with 19 cells are §5 routes (369); rows with 13 cells are §6 WebSocket rows (4). The post-erratum AMI re-parses to row content identical to the pre-erratum parse.
2. Parse permissions and grants from `007_5B.sql`, `096_5B2.sql` and `104_5B3.sql` with:
   - permissions: `\('([a-z_]+:[a-z_]+)',\s*'[^']*',\s*'[a-z_]+',\s*'[a-z_]+'\)`
   - grants: `\('(OWNER|ADMIN|MEMBER|BILLING_ADMIN|VIEWER)',\s*'([a-z_]+:[a-z_]+)'\)`
3. Classify every row (`gen_matrix.py`) by the rules of §7, with the four owner decisions encoded in the generator. Emit §5, §6, §13, §14 and §16 verbatim, with generator assertions. No generated row is edited after generation.
4. Run the sweeps: A — same-class sweeps (`sweep.py`, `sweep2.py`, `sweep3.py`) over 6A–6M plus Gate D; B — the independent grant recomputation of Gate E; C — the consistency sweep (`sweep4.py`) over §13 and the sources plus Gates F–K; D — the high-risk-class sweep (`sweepD.py`), which re-derives each high-risk row from its owning 6X contract and the executed grants.
5. Assemble (`assemble.py`): fill this template from the live repository — git blob hashes, `git diff -U0` against `17ea701`, the parsed matrix, the lifecycle invariants re-derived from the live source lines and the fresh sweep captures — compute every gate, the findings ledger and the adversarial verification, then write this file. Running the assembler twice on unchanged inputs produces byte-identical output.
6. Re-verify the written bytes with an independent validator (`validate.py`) that re-parses this file and the live sources from scratch, sharing no code with the generator or the assembler. Its checks are derived from the document, so its count is not fixed here; its outcome is reported to the reviewer with the run, not embedded in this file.
7. Exercise the validator with a mutation harness (`mutate.py`, 18 generic mutation classes plus the focused lifecycle-race and platform-admin job-resolution mutations) applied to copies of this file and of the live sources in a temporary directory outside the repository, which is deleted after the run. Its outcome is likewise reported with the run. No outcome of the validator or of the harness is written into this document: doing so would make the document depend on bytes it changes.
8. All scripts run with `python3 -B` in a session scratch directory outside the repository. None is committed.

### 20.3 Sweep results

The same-class sweeps compared every contract test/matrix cell that names a system role, an exact permission and an allow/deny result against the parsed grants. They covered three table shapes (role in the first column, role as a column, permission as a column) plus inline role lists.

- Cells compared: 20 + 198 + 67 = 285.
- Contradictions with the grants: **1**, namely 6B L2329 (AAM-F-01, resolved by precedence).
- The list-shape heuristic considered 508 rows and compared 67 cells, with 0 hits. Earlier passes recorded 14 + 13 + 233 = 260 cells compared and 4 list-shape false positives in 6L L1209–1212; the sweeps have since been widened (more table shapes and role-column tables are recognised) and the list heuristic tightened, so these figures are the fresh ones and supersede the earlier counts. The single contradiction is unchanged.

Consistency sweep (`sweep4.py`, fresh run):

```

source lines mentioning break-glass scanned for write-access claims                        checked=185    contradictions=0

key ALLOW only on 6B-018/6B-027                                                            checked=369    contradictions=0
actor type: SELF/PREAUTH never gated by custom-role permissions                            checked=369    contradictions=0
sensitive media (perm usage, holders OWNER/ADMIN only, key scope, PA only via 6M-008/009)  checked=4      contradictions=0
internal routes (exactly 4; public token/key/human PA → 401)                               checked=369    contradictions=0
callback routes (exactly 5; bearer never callback auth)                                    checked=369    contradictions=0
cross-tenant 404 on every RBAC row                                                         checked=369    contradictions=0
```

Sweep D — high-risk classes (`sweepD.py`, fresh run). For every §13 row in a high-risk class (organisation lifecycle, roles, API keys, member suspend/role change, billing, sensitive media, workflow execution, quotas, `/auth/authorize`, jobs, sessions, platform financial/audit), the sweep locates the route in its **owning** 6X document, extracts the permission tokens stated there (following "same as §X.Y" references and, for PA rows, the cited section), and compares them with the §13 permission cell. Each simple RBAC row's role cells are recomputed from the executed grants, and AMI-6A-001 is checked against the AAM-OD-04 C contract text. The registry trace follows AMI-6A-001 → registry entry (6A L488) → producer AMI rows → `view_permission` (catalogue and owning read route) → initiator support → tenant source (migration table with `organization_id`, or the 6J L581 Redis record); for connection sync it also re-derives the record lifecycle (§7.3c) directly from 6J L581 and 6A L486/L488 with its own phrase set, independent of the assembler's gate ZB. The sweep does not read the matrix's own assumptions as evidence. A mutation run (three defects injected into a copy of §13: an ADMIN cell, a permission token, and the 6A-001 `C-JOB` cell) was caught 3/3 before this pass.

The final remediation pass adds the `PLATFORM_ADMIN` generic-job resource-resolution trace: 24 checks, all read from live 6A L488 rule (8a), live 6J L581, the executed `087_5B1.sql` grant table and the generated matrix rows, proving that the candidate organization comes from the stored break-glass grant, that the lookup is confined to `jobauth:{grant.organization_id}:{job_id}`, and that no `SCAN jobauth:*:{job_id}`, organization iteration or global reverse index is permitted; if the resolution order ever became circular again these checks fail. Issues: 0.

# API AUTHORIZATION MATRIX = READY FOR INDEPENDENT REVIEW
